Pricing Logic Reference

Why this tab exists
Every project uses the same workbook template, so the calculation mechanics repeat. This tab holds them once. Folder tabs link back here and only call out exceptions.
  1. Terms used
  2. The formula chain, step by step
  3. Known variations across folders so far
  4. Standard boilerplate (Exclusions, BidCalc authority)

Terms used

The vocabulary that recurs across every folder's own tabs, defined once here so individual folder sections don't need to repeat it:

TermMeaning
MD / SD CaseMulti-Deck / Single-Deck -- standard open refrigerated case (several shelves / one shelf), no doors.
8' / 12'The two standard modular section lengths refrigerated cases are manufactured in.
Evaporator coilThe internal cold-side coil inside a case or walk-in box.
RC / GCRefrigeration Contractor (Climate Pros) / General Contractor.
ST / OT / DTStraight Time / Overtime / Doubletime -- three pay buckets for the same labor hour.
BurdenThe extra cost on top of a worker's base wage covering payroll tax, workers' comp, insurance, benefits -- not profit. Texas burden = 2.0x, so a $34/hr Journeyman really costs $68/hr. Lives in the LABOR RATES tab, varies by region.
Labor BlendFixed % split of total project hours across 5 worker roles (Foreman/Startup/Journeyman/Apprentice/Tradesman) -- a staffing policy input, not derived from drawings.
MarginCalculated as % of sell price, not cost: Sell = Cost ÷ (1−margin%), NOT Cost x (1+margin%). Varies by category and by project -- see section below.
XLOOKUPExcel function used everywhere in this workbook: search a list, return the matching value from another column.
Region / Local codeShort code (e.g. "TX") selecting the correct row in the LABOR RATES wage table.
"REFERENCE ONLY" blocksSome SUMMARY-tab sections are labeled "Do NOT Adjust" -- they mirror a calculation for sanity-checking but never drive the final bid.
BidCalc"Bid Calculator" -- the one tab that is the actual final answer. Every other tab feeds into it; it sums Labor + Materials + Rentals + Subcontractors + Contingency + Warranty + Tax into one Total Bid number. Always the authoritative tab, never a REFERENCE ONLY mirror.
HFGHanging materials, Fittings and Welding Gas -- a PIPE 2.0 cost factor for pipe support/brazing consumables.
Responsibility Matrix (RM)A table assigning each equipment/task to a responsible party (RC, BASC, GC, OEM) -- who furnishes it, who installs it. Present as a real drawing sheet on some projects (Walmart), absent on others (United Market), in which case the workbook's own boilerplate Exclusions list is the fallback.
RD / RE / BASRSheet-series codes seen on drawing sets: RD = Refrigeration Demolition, RE = Refrigeration Electrical, BASR = Building Automation System - Refrigeration (controls).
SubletWork subcontracted to another trade (Sheet Metal, Electrical, Plumbing, Carpentry, Sprinklers) instead of Climate Pros' own crew -- has its own rate table on every workbook, used or not.
Scheduling Data / DispatchA SUMMARY-tab block estimating crew size and job duration from total labor hours, plus dispatch flags per trade (R/EMS/C/M) -- present on every workbook, whether or not it resolves cleanly.

The formula chain, step by step (generic -- applies to every folder)

1
Quantity in, typed by the estimator
A count of cases/coils/equipment items, sourced from a drawing schedule or a scope letter, or an aggregate judgment call when neither cleanly states a total.
2
XLOOKUP converts quantity into material $ and labor hours
Every category (Display Cases, Evaporators, Equipment) has its own small rate table; the formula is always qty x XLOOKUP(type, rate table).
3
Hours classified as ST / OT / DT
Sometimes automatic, sometimes a manual estimator choice (e.g. a "night work" clause pushing everything into Overtime).
4
Hours blended across 5 roles, by a per-project %
Foreman / Startup / Journeyman / Apprentice / Tradesman -- a staffing-policy input.
5
Each role's rate = base wage x burden, with additive OT/DT loading
ST cost-rate = base x (burden+0) OT cost-rate = base x (burden+0.5) DT cost-rate = base x (burden+1) Minneapolis region code "539" is a hardcoded exception: +1/+2 instead of +0.5/+1
6
Cost becomes Sell via reverse-margin
Sell = Cost ÷ (1 - margin%) NOT Cost x (1+margin%)

Naming trap confirmed on HEB 57: BidCalc's own "Markup" column shows a different number than SUMMARY's "Margin %" column for the exact same dollars -- e.g. Margin 25% shows up in BidCalc as Markup 33.3%. Same economics: markup-on-cost = margin/(1-margin). Not a discrepancy, just two tabs describing the same relationship differently -- don't treat a mismatched-looking percentage as an error without checking which convention that tab uses.

7
Tax: category is automatic, rate is always manual
Tax category comes from an XLOOKUP on State + Construction-Type into the TAX TABLE tab. The tax rate is always typed by hand after the estimator manually Googles it -- a HYPERLINK formula literally opens the search, with a note warning to verify multiple results. This is the one universally manual step in an otherwise formula-driven chain, on every folder checked so far.
8
Everything rolls into BidCalc
Labor + Materials + Rentals + Subcontractors + Contingency + Warranty + Tax, each with its own category-specific margin, summed into one Total Bid.

Standard boilerplate present on every workbook

A default Exclusions list -- mostly fixed, but not always identical
The same 13-item list appeared byte-identical on six of the seven folders checked so far: General Contracting, Permits and Fees, Engineering/Drawings, EMS, Seismic work, Local municipality requirements, Roof/floor/wall openings, Trenching/concrete/backfill, Case refurbishing/repair, Case transport/storage/disposal, Plumbing and case condensates, Electrical, Line covers/flashing. Walmart 0344-1005 was the lone exception, adding a 14th item, "Structural/Steel Work" -- plausibly because that job's scope genuinely involves structural work to mount new condenser frames; Trader Joe's #455 and Sam's Club 8250-1010 (both of which also involve real structural/mechanical work) stayed at 13 items regardless. So this list is a default that can be edited per project, but usually isn't even when the scope would justify it -- worth checking the exact wording on every future folder rather than assuming it matches. HEB 383 introduces a second kind of exception: instead of adding a 14th item, its copy is cut short after the 7th item (General Contracting through Roof/floor/wall openings) -- the remaining 6 (Trenching/backfill, Case refurbishing, Case transport, Plumbing/condensates, Electrical, Line covers) are simply absent, not overridden. Some of those (the case-related ones) plausibly don't apply to a case-less ice room; others (Electrical, Line covers) have no obvious reason to be missing. Joe V's #831 repeats the exact same 7-item truncation -- confirming this is a real, recurring template variant rather than a one-off unique to HEB 383, even though Joe V's #831 is a full-scope job where the case-related exclusions would clearly apply.
BidCalc is always authoritative; SUMMARY's own labor breakdown never is
Every workbook checked so far has a "LABOR COST BREAKDOWN (REFERENCE ONLY) -- Do NOT Adjust" block on the SUMMARY tab. It's a sanity-check mirror. BidCalc is the tab that actually produces the number quoted to the customer.
A template-wide formula bug: Contingency Factor's sell price ignores its own margin cell
Confirmed on six separate folders now (HEB Corp #458, Walmart 0344-1005, Trader Joe's #455, Sam's Club 8250-1010, HEB 383, and Joe V's #831), with margin percentages typed into the Contingency Factor's own margin cell repeating mostly at 5% (5%, 3.5%, 5%, 5%, 5%, and 5% again). In every case, the cost is correctly computed off that cell, but the sell value is computed with a hardcoded divisor (cost ÷ 0.8, equivalent to a flat 25% markup every time) instead of referencing the same cell used for cost. Two other rows (Project Management Fee, Performance Bond) carry the identical hardcoded-divisor pattern. This is a defect baked into the template itself, not a one-off data-entry mistake -- it will inflate the sell price on any future folder where Contingency, Project Management Fee, or Performance Bond actually carries a non-default margin.
The HVAC tab's own header block is internally misaligned
Confirmed on six separate folders now (HEB Corp #458, Walmart 0344-1005, Trader Joe's #455, Sam's Club 8250-1010, HEB 383, and Joe V's #831): the HVAC tab's header labels and values are shifted out of alignment relative to each other (e.g. "BID DATE:" displaying the project city, "SCOPE:" displaying "Texas", "DRAWINGS DATE:" displaying the scope text, "REVISIONS #:" displaying a raw date serial number). Harmless every time because the tab itself is entirely unused, but it's a template-level copy-paste artifact, not a project fact -- don't read anything into it if it shows up again.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
Remote Condensing Unit (RCU)A small, standalone transcritical CO2 (R744) condensing unit serving one specific fixture, rather than a full multi-case rack. This job installs two, tagged CU1 and CU2, each dedicated to a single new case.
KE2 / CC200 (Cold Chain Connect)Two distinct, named case-controller BAS platforms both appearing in this folder's controls matrix, each with its own commissioning method: KE2 is set up via WiFi and the manufacturer's own webpages; CC200 is set up via the "Cold Chain Connect" mobile app. The first folder checked so far naming more than one controls platform.
Sam's Master SpecificationSam's Club's own CSI-style spec numbering, distinct from (though conceptually similar to) Walmart's own numbering seen on other folders. Section 13810 = Building Automation System; Section 15600 = Refrigeration Systems.
Suction Sub-Loop / Liquid Sub-Loop BranchA piping topology term from this job's refrigeration detail sheets: a secondary header running between clusters of cases, itself branching off the main suction/liquid lines, used to organize multi-case circuits.
Walk-in Leak Detection ZoneA refrigerant leak-monitoring boundary drawn around one or more walk-in boxes; this job's details specify one combined liquid/suction valve station per zone.

The Uploads -- 13 documents

Two spec excerpts (BAS Section 13810; Refrigeration Systems Section 15600) set the rules, followed by two Responsibility Matrix sheets and eight drawing sheets. RM1 repeats the same 57-item numbered scope-of-work matrix seen on the Walmart folders, byte-identical down to the legend (RC/GC/BAS/OEM/PS/WMT/AES/WTC/MC) and the contact emails (WMSE@novar.com, WalmartSupport@Copeland.com, RMSupport@Walmart.com, MCEquip@Walmart.com). RM1.1 adds two more matrices: a "Case/Walk-In BAS Programming & Configuration Matrix" (introducing the KE2 and CC200 controller platforms) and a "Walk-In Box Projects" responsibility matrix covering Batten & Seal, Reline, and Replace-Evaporator/Box scenarios.

A document that says "Walmart" inside a Sam's Club job
RM1's own boilerplate note reads: "...READ AND USED IN CONJUNCTION WITH ADDITIONAL, DETAILED INFORMATION IN THE WALMART PROJECT DRAWINGS AND SPECIFICATIONS..." -- not a copy-paste coincidence between similar templates, but literally the same corporate document referencing Walmart by name inside a Sam's Club project. Combined with the identical legend, contacts, and (per the drawing details) the same "Quick Refrigeration" salvage vendor pattern seen on a Walmart folder, this confirms Sam's Club and Walmart share one corporate refrigeration-program template, not just a similar one.

The eight remaining sheets are Henderson Engineers drawings (job #2450005143; drawn by JJG, checked LAW/CK): two Refrigeration Demolition plans (RD1/RD2, tagging roughly 20 cases relocated and 18-21 demoed per sheet, plus a "SHOP-IN Dairy Cooler" box with its own case tags), three Refrigeration Plans (R1-R3, confirming the R744/CU1/CU2 tags), and two Refrigeration Schedules (R4/R5) that name every rack directly: three existing halocarbon racks staying in service (LTA and MTB on R404A, MTC on R448A) plus two brand-new standalone Carrier R744 transcritical CO2 units, CU1 and CU2, each serving one new fixture (a Cafe Cooler and a Grab-and-Go case). Two generic detail sheets (R6/R7) provide Henderson's standard DX and TCO2 piping/valve/support details, including a CO2 charging-station detail rated to 870 PSIG service pressure and 4,000 PSIG hose burst rating.

Tab 1 -- CP Est Pro 2.0 SUMMARY

1.1 Header

McAllen, Texas. Scope "Major Remodel." Labor division "TX Valley, TX." Estimator: Jay Jarratt Jr. Warranty Contractor: Climate Pros. "Checked By" not required -- sell price is under $1M.

1.2 Display Cases

19 new 12' MD Cases installed, 41 12' cases relocated, 13 cases removed. Same rate-lookup table described in Pricing Logic Reference.

MD (new): 19 x $130 = $2,470 material | 133 pipefitter hrs | 95 case-setter hrs Temp/Relocate: 41 x $75 = $3,075 material | 123 pipefitter hrs | 205 case-setter hrs Remove: 13 EA, $0 material | 13 pipefitter hrs | 13 case-setter hrs TOTALS row: 60 cases (19 new + 41 relocated; Remove doesn't add to this count) | $5,545 material | 269 pipefitter hrs | 313 case-setter hrs

All three numbers trace exactly to BidCalc's own scope text: "Install New Cases (19)," "Relocate Cases (41)," "Demo Existing Cases (13)."

1.3 Evaporators

13 Commercial coils + 21 Industrial coils = 34 total. 13 x $30 + 21 x $55 = $1,545 material, 13 x 12 + 21 x 16 = 492 hrs. The 21 Industrial figure matches the scope's "Install New Coils (13) + (8) Relocated Industrial" exactly (13 new + 8 relocated).

1.5 IN-HOUSE GC and SUBLET CONTRACTORS

Same fixed rate table as every other folder -- see Pricing Logic Reference. Entirely zero hours across Sheet Metal, Electrical, Plumbing, Carpentry, G.C., and Sprinklers; no subcontracted trades on this job.

1.6 Refrigerant pricing table -- a real, sizeable pricing gap

1,337.36 lbs total, split across four rows -- but a large share of it sits unpriced:

EQUIPMENT tab refrigerant: 40 lbs + PIPE 2.0 piping refrigerant: 895.3556 lbs + Cases installed/relocated x 5: 60 x 5 = 300 lbs + Evaporator coils x 3: 34 x 3 = 102 lbs = 1,337.3556 lbs total

Of that total, only 800 lbs got manually reassigned to a priced row: 600 lbs to R507/404A at $17/lb ($10,200) and 200 lbs to R744 CO2 at $6/lb ($1,200). The remaining 537.36 lbs -- 40% of the whole charge -- sits in the default R407F bucket at $0/lb, contributing nothing to the $11,400 Total Refrigerant Cost.

The largest version of the "$0/lb default bucket" gap seen so far
On other folders this default-bucket leftover was either the whole charge (correctly, when no refrigerant was priced) or a rounding artifact of a few thousandths of a pound. Here it's 537.36 real pounds -- roughly 40% of the job's total refrigerant weight -- left unpriced. At even the R507/404A rate used elsewhere on this same table ($17/lb), that gap represents over $9,100 of refrigerant value the price sheet is not charging for.

Full walkthrough: the two new standalone CO2 units

The architecturally distinctive fact of this folder, traced end to end
Unlike a full rack conversion, this job adds CO2 in the smallest possible increment -- two single-fixture units -- while three existing halocarbon racks keep running untouched.
1
The Refrigeration Schedule names both units directly
Uploads: R4/R5
Carrier R744 transcritical "Remote Condensing Units," tagged CU1 and CU2, each serving one fixture (a Cafe Cooler and a Grab-and-Go case) -- alongside three existing racks (LTA/MTB on R404A, MTC on R448A) that the schedule confirms are staying in service.
2
EQUIPMENT tab prices them via the closest catalog match
EQUIPMENT!row 11
"1/4-10 HP Condensing Units," qty 2 -- $200 material, 32 labor hrs, 40 lbs refrigerant.
3
PARTS tab buys the CO2-rated hardware they need
PARTS!rows 103-115
8x 1/2" Mueller 140 BAR XHP CO2 ball valves, plus 4x smaller CO2 ball valves w/ integral pressure relief -- hardware a standard DX unit would never need.
4
COPPER_INSULATION_FORM orders the high-pressure line itself
COPPER_INSULATION_FORM!rows 58-59
560 LF of XHP/90-BAR copper (160 LF at 3/8" + 400 LF at 1/2") -- the physical pipe connecting the charging station to CU1/CU2, per the R7 charging-station detail.
5
The 40 lbs of CO2 refrigerant feeds the project total, then BidCalc
SUMMARY refrigerant table / BidCalc
40 lbs is one of four terms summing to 1,337.36 lbs total charge (though only 200 of the project's eventual R744 lbs get priced -- see the refrigerant-gap callout in Section 1.6). Final Total Bid $868,937.

What's unique about this folder

The first hybrid/incremental refrigeration architecture seen
Every prior refrigeration-scope folder was either a full conversion (halocarbon to CO2), a pure gas swap, or a like-for-like condenser replacement. This one keeps three existing halocarbon racks running untouched while adding CO2 in the smallest possible increment -- two standalone, single-fixture Remote Condensing Units. The PARTS tab shows this split physically: standard DX valves and 140-BAR XHP CO2 valves, ordered side by side on the same sheet.
Two named case-controller platforms in one document
KE2 and Cold Chain Connect (CC200) both appear in the same BAS Programming & Configuration Matrix, each with its own commissioning procedure. Every prior folder with a named controls platform (Novar) named only one.
The largest unpriced-refrigerant gap found so far
537.36 lbs -- 40% of this job's total refrigerant charge -- sits in the workbook's $0/lb default bucket, never manually reassigned to a priced row. Worth flagging to the team as a real dollar exposure, not just a template quirk.

Folder: Trader Joe's #455 -- San Antonio (North), TX

26.06.17 -- Remodel with CO2 (Transcritical) Upgrade

  16. What's unique about this folder

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
Transcritical CO2A refrigeration architecture using CO2 (R744) as the sole refrigerant at pressures above its critical point on the discharge side. Requires a Gas Cooler instead of a condenser, and a Booster Rack (two-stage compression) instead of a standard rack.
Gas Cooler / Booster (Flex) RackThe two new rooftop units this job installs -- a Baltimore Air Coil TrilliumSeries Gas Cooler (3,244 lb) and a CO2 Booster Rack (6,000 lb), confirmed by name in the structural calc, the electrical panel schedule, and the price sheet's own EQUIPMENT tab.
MezzanineThe new elevated steel platform built specifically to hold the Gas Cooler and Booster Rack -- engineered against local wind/seismic/snow loads in a dedicated structural calculation package.
MCC / SCCMaster Case Controller / Slave Case Controller -- the EMS network hierarchy for refrigerated case control, daisy-chained by system tag (e.g. MT13, LT05).
CRLDSA refrigerant leak detector model (809-1214) rated 0-10,000 ppm, specifically sized for CO2 detection in cooler/freezer walk-ins.
URI (United Refrigeration Inc.)The refrigerant reclaim vendor named directly in this folder's price sheet and drawings -- a different mechanism from the "Rapid Recovery" vendor seen on Walmart folders, suggesting the reclaim vendor is customer-specific, not a fixed template default.

The Uploads -- four separate document packages, not one

Two PDFs, but they actually bundle four distinct packages from four different firms -- a first for this project. A dedicated Structural Engineering Calculations package (Brad Young & Associates, 84 pages) justifies a new rooftop platform, confirming two real equipment weights: a CO2 Gas Cooler (3,244 lb) and a CO2 Booster Rack (6,000 lb), engineered against San Antonio's 108 mph wind and low seismic loads.

The second, 54-page PDF opens with a conventional architectural/electrical/plumbing permit set (Sargenti Architects) -- Demolition, Construction/Fixture, Electrical (including a dedicated Refrigeration Power Plan and Panel Schedules), and Plumbing sheets. Buried further into the same file are two more packages that don't share the same title-block template, which is why they don't show up in any sheet index: refrigeration case/piping details from a firm called Arctic Cooling Systems (confirming Hillphoenix cases and the Gas Cooler's exact model, Baltimore Air Coil TSDC-CO2-112), and a full EMS controls set from R3 Retail Development (case controller network, leak detection, an Acorn Vacuum System, and a rack emergency shutdown).

The richest cross-document confirmation seen so far
The electrical panel schedule gives real loads for both new units -- Gas Cooler 10.56 kVA, Booster Rack 53.62 kVA -- and the same case-circuit system tags (MT13, MT16, MT19, MT20, MT21, MT24, LT03-LT11) appear identically across the electrical drawings, the EMS drawings, and the price sheet's own PIPE 2.0 systems table.
One likely drafting artifact
A title-block reading "Adjmi Residence, Allenhurst, New Jersey" (a different firm's unrelated job) recurs identically across several EMS pages. Almost certainly a reused CAD template that wasn't fully overwritten, not real project content.

What's unique about this folder

The best cross-document consistency of any folder checked so far
Case-circuit system tags, CO2 valve part numbers, equipment weights, and the full refrigerant formula all trace cleanly between the price sheet and four independently-authored documents -- a structural engineer's calc package, an architect's permit set, a refrigeration vendor's case details, and an EMS controls firm's drawings.
A different refrigerant reclaim mechanism than the Walmart folders
Rapid Recovery -- the vendor named on every Walmart folder checked so far -- is unused here. Refrigerant reclaim instead runs through United Refrigeration Inc. directly, named in both the price sheet and the drawings. The reclaim vendor appears to be set by the customer, not baked into the template.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
Cap-XCapital Expenditure -- Walmart's classification for this project type. The source document lists the full family: COMBO, REF-1 through REF-4, HVAC-1 through HVAC-4, each with different jobsite signage requirements.
VFDVariable Frequency Drive -- controls condenser fan speed. Central to this job: one VFD per condenser, replaced or newly installed, governed by a written Sequence of Operations on the BASR1 sheet.
IPRInlet Pressure Regulator -- an electronic valve at the condenser outlet that maintains minimum head pressure. Wired back to the rack controller.
50/50 Split CondenserA condenser design that can isolate half its fan bank in cold weather. This job's general notes specifically require standardizing older "50/25/25" configurations to 50/50.
Suction Group ReportA report the refrigeration contractor must request by email before installing equipment or adjusting rack parameters -- a required pre-work step, not a document already in hand.
OEM SubmittalThe equipment manufacturer's own as-built documentation. Unusually, this folder's submittal sheets are the original 2003 package for this store, kept alive with revision markups ever since -- not a fresh drawing.

The Uploads -- a complete drawing set, with a 20-year paper trail

Two combined PDFs (5 and 11 pages); together they cover all nine sheets in the package's own Index of Drawings -- the first folder where nothing referenced is actually missing: C1 (title), RM1 (a 57-item Responsibility Matrix), BASR1/BASR2 (BAS wiring + sequence of operations), R1 (demo/replacement plan), R2 (condenser equipment schedule), R3/R3.1 (refrigeration submittals), RE1 (electrical plans).

R2/R3 aren't freshly drafted -- they're the original 2003 Hill Phoenix OEM submittal for this exact store, carrying a live revision log through today: 2012 (spare-circuit change), 2023 ("changes made per EOR specifications [Hussmann]"), 9/30/25 ("condenser replacement, compressor upgrade recommended but not allowed in SOW"), and 5/17/26 ("updated to 2026 condenser specifications") -- that last revision is what actually governs this bid. It names real equipment: three Hill Phoenix condensers (models BNH-D06-A051 and BNH-D08-A068), refrigerant R-404A, Carlyle reciprocating compressors running a specified POE oil.

Two confirmed citations for night work, not one
The source document's General Requirements state outright that "all Cap-X/special refrigeration projects are to be bid as night work," and BidCalc's own scope text separately says "All Work Figured Night Work." Every hour classified as Overtime on this job traces to an explicit sentence, not an assumption.

RM1 assigns all 57 numbered scope items to a specific party (RC/GC/BAS/OEM/WTC) and also names two vendors by contact: AES Industries for hauling away the decommissioned condensers -- with an explicit instruction not to include any salvage credit in the bid -- and United Refrigeration for refrigerant recovery under Walmart's banking program.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
SC CaseSelf-Contained Case -- has its own built-in compressor, never connects to a central rack. Confirmed by name in the source document, which is why this line prices at $0 material and only startup labor.
Porta PackTemporary, portable refrigeration used to hold product cold mid-remodel while permanent racks are swapped out. Not named in the source document, but implied by context.
HR TankHeat Reclaim Tank -- stores refrigerant heat reclaimed for water heating. The source document covers heat-reclaim valves/diverting logic but never itemizes the tank itself.
CxACommissioning Authority -- a new role in this document: leads the commissioning plan, reviews design documents, coordinates functional tests, maintains the Issues Log.
Edge XT (rack brand)Name given to the 4 new racks in the scope text. Not found in the source document's approved-manufacturer lists (Hussmann, Hill Phoenix, Zero Zone) -- plausibly Hill Phoenix, unconfirmed.

The Uploads -- specification manual

One file: 02 HEB CARTHAGE RRP SPECS.pdf. Unlike every other folder's Uploads, this is not a drawing package at all -- it's a written CSI/MasterFormat-style specification manual, Division 00 through Division 33. Most of it (Divisions 00-01 contract administration, and the bulk of Divisions 02-33 covering concrete, steel, drywall, plumbing fixtures, sitework paving, etc.) is generic H-E-B corporate boilerplate that would appear in the spec book for any H-E-B store -- confirmed by reading Section 01 10 00 "Summary" in full, which contains zero project-specific facts. Three sections are actually relevant to refrigeration: Walk-In Coolers and Freezers (confirms the boxes are Owner-furnished, installed by a separate "H-E-B Box Vendor" -- not Climate Pros' scope); Division 20 Refrigeration (refrigerant compliance/recordkeeping, condensing units, evaporator coils, CO2 Cascade & Parallel Compressor Racks, field installation rules, evacuation/charging, commissioning, startup checklists, control sequences); and Refrigerant Piping (a generic copper/steel piping and valve spec written around R-410A air-conditioning applications -- tangential to the main rack scope in Division 20).

Every technical requirement defers to documents that are not in this Uploads folder
The text promises three documents that are never present: a Responsibility Matrix, a refrigeration schedule, and a Color Coded Store Fixture Plan. No drawing set, floor plan, equipment schedule, or as-built sheet index exists anywhere in this package -- this specification contains zero project-specific facts about what Carthage actually has.

1.6 Refrigerant pricing table -- a four-part formula

Price-per-lb by refrigerant type (R449A $10.25/lb, R22 $45/lb, R507/404A $18/lb, R744 CO2 $4/lb, etc.). This job charges 3,862.27 lbs entirely as R449A -- zero CO2, zero R22. The total isn't a typed number; it's a formula pulling from four different places at once:

PIPE 2.0 piping refrigerant: 418.27 lbs + Display Cases count x 5: 57 x 5 = 285 lbs + Evaporator coils x 3: 13 x 3 = 39 lbs + EQUIPMENT tab refrigerant: 3,120 lbs = 3,862.27 lbs total x $10.25/lb = $39,588.28

Of that total, the EQUIPMENT tab's 3,120 lbs is almost entirely the 3 temporary "Porta Pack" units (3,000 lbs) -- meaning temporary bridge refrigeration outweighs the 4 new permanent racks' own charge (120 lbs) by 25 to 1 on this job.

RENTALSFreight/Disposal, Forklift, Crane, Rapid Recovery, Scissor Lifts, Container -- itemizedFreight $2,500 + Forklift $1,450 + Crane $10,500 + Rapid Recovery $7,600 + Scissor Lifts $4,750 + Container $1,000 = $27,800 total
Out of Town SubsistenceTotal Hours ÷ 8 = days, x per-day hotel/per-diem/tolls rate415 days computed, $0/day rate -- formula exists, not materially used
Pricing logic: Rapid Recovery traces to a real specification requirement
The specification's Refrigerant Compliance section names Rapid Recovery, with a specific contact, as the required vendor for recovering R-22 from existing systems. This job's new refrigerant is entirely R449A -- so the $7,600 Rapid Recovery line only makes sense as recovering R-22 from the 4 old racks being demolished, even though nothing in either document states that outright.

1.11 Labor Cost Breakdown

Labeled "Do NOT Adjust" -- a sanity-check mirror, not authoritative. BidCalc is (see Pricing Logic Reference). Total Labor: $271,796.34.

1.12 Total Summary -- and a margin label that doesn't match its own dollars

Margin is set per category, not one flat number across the whole bid. Parts Sheet Buyouts, Permits, Subsistence, and In-House GC all carry their standard 20%/30%/20% margins too, but stay at $0 on this job:

CategoryCostSellMargin
Labor$271,796.34$357,626.7624%
Materials$200,210.84$263,435.3224%
Rentals$27,800$34,75020%
Sublet Sheet Metal$1,890$2,362.5020%
Fixed 1% Warranty$7,670.24$7,670.24--
Contingency Factor$34,640.77$43,300.96labeled 5%
Sales Tax (8.25%, Sales Tax Labor & Mats)$57,871.73$57,871.73--
TOTAL$601,879.91$767,018Gross Profit $165,138.09 / 21.53%
Formula bug Contingency Factor's sell price ignores its own margin cell
Contingency's cost ($34,640.77) is correctly derived from its own 5% margin cell. But its sell value ($43,300.96) is computed with a hardcoded ÷0.8 -- a fixed 20% markup -- instead of referencing that same 5% cell. The displayed "5%" and the actual dollars disagree by design. Two other rows (Project Management Fee, Performance Bond) carry the identical hardcoded-divisor pattern; both are $0 on this job so it doesn't show up in the total, but it would inflate the sell price the moment either line is actually used.

Tab 6 -- EQUIPMENT SALES BUYOUTS

All rows blank (unused this job) except a Freight row (qty 1, $0). Independent pricing logic: Tax: 0.085 (8.5%) -- a different rate than the main SUMMARY tax (8.25%). Total Sell Price: $0.

Tab 7 -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM

Type L copper spans all nine standard sizes (3/8" through 2-5/8" ACR, 160-1,000 LF each); Type K entirely unused. Type M: 3/4"=160 LF + 1"=80 LF, with Black Saddles at three sizes (214.4/97.6/8 qty). XHP, 90 BAR rows are entirely zero across every size -- confirming this job has no high-pressure CO2 piping anywhere, consistent with the refrigerant table's zero R744 charge.

Tab 8 -- HVAC

Every row checked -- entirely zero, as expected for a refrigeration-only remodel. One cosmetic quirk: this tab's own header block has its field labels and values shifted out of alignment (e.g. "BID DATE:" shows "Carthage," "SCOPE:" shows "Texas") -- a copy-paste artifact in the workbook, harmless since the tab is unused, but worth flagging as a template bug rather than a project fact.

Tab 9 -- TAX TABLE

Static, unchanged. Texas's row: "Sales Tax Labor & Mats" for Existing Construction (this Remodel), but "Use Tax Mats Only" for New Construction -- the table's own header note confirms Texas and Arizona are the only two states in the entire 50-state list where the two construction types resolve to different categories.

Tab 10 -- BidCalc

Same 10-tab rollup structure. The Description/Scope cell (rows 1-2) carries the full bulleted scope summary quoted throughout this section. Total Bid Tax Minus Equip: $57,871.73 -- traced to the Sales Tax row (1.12). Markup shown here (0.3158 on labor/materials, 0.25 on rentals/sublet/contingency) is the same margin numbers from Tab 1, just expressed as cost-based markup instead of sell-based margin -- see Pricing Logic Reference, step 6.

What's unique about this folder

The Uploads contain no project-specific refrigeration data at all
Every other document set reviewed so far has included at least some real drawing content, even when incomplete. This one is purely a written specification -- rules for how to build and document a refrigeration system, with the drawing, schedule, and matrix documents it explicitly depends on all absent. Every quantity in the price sheet is self-sourced.
A halocarbon-only refrigerant load, dominated by temporary equipment
Despite the source document's largest section covering CO2 cascade rack systems in detail, this job runs entirely on R449A -- and the temporary "Porta Pack" refrigeration used mid-remodel (3,000 lbs) outweighs the permanent new equipment's own charge (120 lbs) by 25 to 1.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
WICPWalk-In Control Panel -- provided by H-E-B via the EMS vendor, mounted/powered by the Electrical Contractor, everything else on the panel done by the Refrigeration Contractor.
RDMThe brand of "Data Manager" controller H-E-B standardizes on -- provided by H-E-B, installed by Electrical, control-wiring coordinated by RC with the Data Contractor.
EEPRElectronic Evaporator Pressure Regulator -- a 24V control valve, wiring by RC.
SA / RA / DA / LS / LI / MS / TS / ESSensor/alarm symbol codes on H-E-B refrigeration drawings: Supply Air temp, Return Air temp, Door Alarm, Leak Sensor, Leak Indicator, magnetic roof-hatch (Motion/roof) Sensor, Temperature Sensor, freezer Entrapment Switch. All provided by the EMS vendor, terminated by RC.
Plan Modification (addendum)H-E-B's name for an addendum letter -- "Plan Modification 18" here, issued by GPM Engineering.
KeynoteA numbered callout on a drawing sheet (e.g. "Keynote #4 on MEPR-1.1") that the price sheet can cite directly by number -- a precise traceability mechanism.
R449AA refrigerant type priced at $10.25/lb on this job; see Pricing Logic Reference for how refrigerant type/price varies by folder.

How to read this section

Each numbered tab below follows the same shape: what the section is, the real numbers in it, the formula behind each number (dark code block, exactly as it appears in Excel), and where the input came from. A Scope tag means traced to a specific Upload sentence; Manual means the estimator typed it with no traceable source; Mismatch flags a place where Excel and Uploads disagree or don't reconcile. Cell references like F21 are real coordinates -- open United Market Street #502 Lubbock TX 06.04.26 - Refrg SOW.xlsx (password noZZel) and follow along.

Terms this folder has

Every term this folder introduced turned out to be universal to the workbook template -- see the Pricing Logic Reference tab for the full glossary (MD/SD Case, Burden, Labor Blend, Margin, XLOOKUP, Region/Local code, REFERENCE ONLY blocks, HFG, BidCalc, and more). This project's specific values: margin 25%, region "TX", burden 2.0x.

The Uploads (source documents)

No refrigeration drawing exists in this package
No R-series plan, no equipment schedule, no responsibility matrix. Just two prose scope letters and two whole-store FF&E fixture plans with no refrigeration system tags. Every quantity in the Price Sheet was typed by the estimator directly from reading prose.

Four documents make up the full Uploads package for this folder:

FileWhat it is
2802-502-Refrigeration Scope.pdfDepartment-by-department case-swap letter (Meat, Dairy, Floral, Bakery, Produce, Frozen Food, Beer & Wine), each bullet naming a refrigeration system ID (A1-A9, B10-B22, C25-27, D29-36, E44-51).
2802-502 Refrigerant Conversion.pdfGas-conversion procedure, R507 → R448a, split into Base Bid (Racks A, B, C) and Alternate Bid (Racks D, E, F).
UN 02802 (0502) Remodelplan ...DEMO.pdfWhole-store FF&E demolition floor plan. Confirms the term "Multi-Deck" (its Comparative Analysis table) but carries no refrigeration system tags.
UN 02802 (0502) Remodelplan ...PROPOSED.pdfCompanion "proposed" FF&E floor plan -- post-remodel fixture layout, same lack of system tags.

Refrigeration Scope letter

page 1
Refrigeration Scope -- Store 2802/502

Dining Room

  • Remove 2 Ice machines from Coke Freestyle machines
  • Install new (2) ice machines on new coke machines, include water and drain lines

Meat Market

  • Change out 44' of fresh meat cases system B20. One for one swap -- use existing lines, verify riser is 1 3/8" (change if needed), reuse sensor cables/control valve
  • Change out 68' of smoked meat & cheese cases systems B18 & B19 -- B18 abandoned/capped, B19 line sets reused, new 7/8" riser required
  • Change out coffins A6 thru A9, one for one swap, reuse existing lines/sensors/control valve
  • Change case skin on (2) 3 door freezer case -- equipment provided by USM
  • Change out evaporator in meat market freezer A10, meat market cooler B21, meat market holding cooler B22, seafood freezer D30, seafood cooler D43

Dairy -- 36' rear load cases system C25; 68' cases systems C26 & C27 (C27 abandoned/capped, C26 reused, new 7/8" riser)

Floral -- remote bucket case system E51A (no sensor cable currently -- needs adding)

pages 2-3

Bakery -- remove 3 medium temp cases, replace with 2 new cases system D36 (extend branch lines, install new ball valves + new CDS-25 valve at each new case); remove 2-door freezer, replace with new 3-door freezer system D29; change out glass doors on bakery freezer vault (doors provided by USM)

Produce -- 36' deck cases E44; 56' deck cases E47 & E48 (install new ball valves); remove 2 refrigerated produce tables E46 (not replaced); remove 8' case at back of store system E47 (not replaced)

Frozen Food -- 20 doors systems A1/A2; remove 5 back-to-back Kysor cases from systems A3/A4/A5, replace with new Hussman RLN cases (13 doors/side + 2-door end cap each end, new overhead line sets from motor room); same pattern for systems B13/B14/B15; 17 doors system B16; 17 doors system B17

Beer & Wine -- change end cap wine case at end of frozen food (on RTU, reuse lines/controls)

page 3-4 -- closing list

General items to include in your bid: line set covers/close-offs, trim install, dispose of removed cases, unloading (forklift), setting/leveling cases, crane service for RTUs, rental equipment, roof penetration repair, refrigerant supply, condensate drains, all piping/insulation/hangers, CPC sensor connections, temperature control/defrost/fan relays, Start Up, name tags on new cases, refrigerant tracking updates.

"All work is night work unless otherwise specified."

Refrigerant Conversion letter

pages 1-2
Refrigerant Conversion Scope -- Store 2802-502

Base Bid: Conversion of Racks A, B, and C. Alternate Bid: Conversion of Racks D, E, and F. Converting from 507 to 448 gas.

  • Survey store; determine system/component compatibility with new refrigerant (TXV powerheads, valve superheat adjustment)
  • Remove oil from compressors/oil separator, replace with new POE oil (provided by USM); replace oil filters
  • After 1 week, test oil for mineral-oil % with a refractometer; repeat oil change if above 5% (20% acceptable on 448a conversions)
  • Remove suction filters if present, replace as needed
  • Leak-check all racks/conventional systems including overhead piping
  • Recover refrigerant via approved recovery service (property of USM); remove/replace seals, O-rings, gaskets on high side; rebuild/replace solenoid valves, ball valves, regulators; replace schrader cores; remove/replace liquid line driers, pull vacuum, charge with new refrigerant (provided by USM)
  • Start-up, adjust high/low pressure controls, adjust EMS settings (leak detection + refrigerant type), adjust EPRs and superheats
  • Label systems, update RCP forms, submit Refrigeration/HVAC System Information Form to RFSC; leak-check schedule: day after, 3 days, 2 weeks, then every 4 weeks for 90 days
  • Warranty: 90 days on refrigerant leaks from this conversion

Whole-store FF&E floor plans (DEMO / PROPOSED)

Confirmed: not refrigeration drawings
Full-store fixture layouts, scale 3/32"=1'-0". Checked both files against every refrigeration system ID named in the scope letter (A1-A9, B10-B22, C25-27, D29-36, E44-51) and found zero matches. They do contain a legend (New/Surplus/Relocated/Existing/Removed/Priority 2 Demo) and a "Comparative Analysis" table using the term "Multi-Deck" (Multi-Deck Produce, Dairy, Meat/Deli, Frozen Food) -- confirms the terminology, contributes nothing to the takeoff.

Tab 1 -- CP Est Pro 2.0 SUMMARY

The main worksheet. Nearly every other tab either feeds this one or is fed by it.

1.1 Header block (rows 3-15)

Manual all typed by hand: Project Name, City (Lubbock), State (Texas), Scope text, Drawing Date, New/Remodel flag, Estimator, Labor division, Quote class, Warranty contractor, Change Order Y/N.

F13: =IF(F5="", "Enter 'Project State'...", IF(ISERROR(O182), "Pending Total Sell Price...", IF(O182>1000000, "Check Required, Insert Name Here", "No check currently required, sell price < $1M"))) → "No check currently required, sell price < $1M"

A self-checking cell: if the eventual sell price exceeds $1,000,000, it changes its own message to demand a manager check.

1.2 Display Cases (rows 19-29)

One row per case type. Estimator types a count under "8'"/"12'" (columns C/D); everything else is a formula reading the rate table to the right (columns I-P).

Case TypeMaterial /8'Material /12'Pipefitter hrs /8'Pipefitter hrs /12'Case-setter hrs /8'Case-setter hrs /12'
MD Cases$104$1307755
SD Cases$88$1106755

Correction to remember
"Pipefitter labor" / "Case-setter labor" columns are HOURS, not dollars -- they only become dollars after the labor-blend/rate-lookup step (Tab 1, section 1.6).
Row 21, MD Cases, 70 typed at 12': E21 = C21+D21 = 70 F21 = XLOOKUP(type,rates,material)*count (70 x $130) = $9,100 G21 = XLOOKUP(type,rates,pipefitter_hrs)*count (70 x 7) = 490 hrs H21 = XLOOKUP(type,rates,casesetter_hrs)*count (70 x 5) = 350 hrs Row 29 TOTALS: Material $9,175 | Pipefitter 565 hrs | Case-setter 427 hrs
Mismatch Where did "70" come from?
Not one clean sentence, and no drawing exists to check against. The scope letter mixes linear feet ("44' of fresh meat cases"), discrete units ("coffins A6 thru A9"), and door counts ("20 doors of frozen food") with no stated conversion rate. 70 is the estimator's own aggregate judgment. See section 15 for the full write-up.

1.3 Evaporators (rows 32-37)

Two coil types, each a fixed material/labor rate that the estimator multiplies by a typed quantity:

Coil TypeMaterialLabor (hrs)
Commercial$3012
Industrial$5516
F34 = XLOOKUP("Commercial",...)*8 = $240 (8 x $30) G34 = XLOOKUP("Commercial",...)*8 = 96 hrs (8 x 12) Row 37 note: "Material is for hanging coils only!"

$30 is far too cheap to be an actual coil -- it's furnished by someone else (owner/case manufacturer); Climate Pros prices only the labor + small hardware to mount it.

Mismatch 8 coils priced, only 5 confirmed in text
Exactly 5 explicit "change out evaporator" bullets exist (meat freezer A10, meat cooler B21, meat holding cooler B22, seafood freezer D30, seafood cooler D43). The remaining 3 are plausibly coils hung inside 3 new-case installs (D29, D36 x2, E51A) but this is inference, not text. See section 15.

1.4 Non-Itemized Equipment Room & RTU Breakdown (rows 39-52)

A handful of miscellaneous line items that don't fit the Display Cases/Evaporators rate tables, each traced to a scope sentence or a formula pulling from another tab:

RowDescriptionMaterialLabor (hrs)Source

Term trap: "Equipment" means 3 different things in this workbook
(1) the EQUIPMENT tab itself, (2) row 41 "Equipment Sheet Totals" pulling #1 in as one line, (3) row 100 in the hours table (1.5), labeled "EQUIPMENT," which pulls the whole $13,760.64/144-hour total from this section -- not just the EQUIPMENT tab.

1.5 Labor hours by task (rows 96-121)

Header row 95: FUNCTION | ST HOURS | OT HOURS | DT HOURS | MATERIALS COST. Each hours cell is a formula pulling from elsewhere on this sheet.

RowFunctionSTOTDTMaterialsPulled from
98Pipe03460$51,302.60='PIPE 2.0'!D3, placed under OT
100Equipment01440$13,760.64=G52 (section 1.4's whole block)
101Case Receive, Set & Uncrate04270$0= Display Cases case-setter total
102Piping in Cases05650$9,175= Display Cases pipefitter total
103Coils0960$240= Evaporators total
121TOTAL HOURS2,384.34--=F119+G119+H119
Discovery: 992 hours classified as Overtime, and the letter explains why
Rows 101/102 place 100% of case-setter (427) and pipefitter (565) hours under OT, not ST -- a manual choice. The scope letter's closing line: "All work is night work unless otherwise specified." Night-shift work commonly triggers overtime pay -- directly traceable, and it matters financially because OT carries a richer burden multiplier below.

1.8 Sales Tax (row 189)

Mechanism: see Pricing Logic Reference, step 7. This job: category = "Sales Tax Labor & Mats" (taxes everything), rate = 0.0825 -- typed by hand, this job's branch = (labor+mat sell+contingency)*rate = $41,519.93.

Mismatch Sourced from neither the Uploads nor any formula
The tax rate is typed in after a manual Google search -- see section 15.

Tab 2 -- EQUIPMENT

A fixed checklist of ~40 possible equipment/repair line items, unchanged across every project. Estimator types a quantity in column B; columns G/H/I are formulas: =B x rate for material/labor/refrigerant, rates stored off-screen in columns M/N/O.

Only 3 non-zero rows on this job (all gas-conversion related, qty 6 each -- one per rack): Row 34: Change Oil Filter (1) OF-303 $660 material / 6 hrs Row 35: Change Rack Main Liquid Drier RC4864HH $780 material / 12 hrs Row 36: Change Rack Suction Filters (8) Cores $2,400 material / 30 hrs Row 43 TOTALS: =SUM(G11:G42) $3,840 material | =SUM(H11:H42) 48 hrs

Scope All three trace directly to the Refrigerant Conversion letter's procedure -- qty 6 = one pass per rack being converted (Base Bid A/B/C + Alternate D/E/F).

Tab 3 -- PIPE 2.0

Not empty on this project, despite most case-swap bullets saying "reuse existing lines." Header stats: Install Labor Hours 346, LF Pipe w/Waste 3,460, Piping Waste 10%, HFG Factor 0.45, HFG Cost $15,146.28, Piping Refrigerant 177.708 lbs.

One real row: 0.75" pipe, 200 LF, Type M copper, insulation 1.125" ID x 1" thick, 96 LF, $302.40, FG part #3183.

3.3 Suction/Discharge & Insulation columns (Q-AF)

Copper suction/discharge line sizing, then Black/White Insulation tables with matching LF/cost/part# shape. These feed COPPER_INSULATION_FORM (Tab 7) directly.

Mismatch Exact $ cost of this piping not fully verified
Columns Q-AF hold real cost-per-LF and part numbers, but I couldn't confidently confirm whether each row-block is a per-system or per-pipe-size aggregation in the time available. See section 15.

Tab 4 -- PARTS

A static catalog, 150+ named parts with FG part numbers and fixed unit prices -- a price book, not recalculated per project.

RowPartQty

2121-1/2" Galvanized Strut, 10ft50

Scope Ball valves trace to "Install new ball valves" (Produce E44, E47/E48). Strut/silphos/heat-tape are standard consumables for the new branch piping identified in Tab 3.

Tab 5 -- LABOR RATES

Static, ~45 rows, one per region: LOCAL code, Foreman/Journeyman/Apprentice/Tradesman base wage, Region name, Burden multiplier, Updated date. This is what every XLOOKUP in SUMMARY and BidCalc reads from.

Row 45 -- Texas: LOCAL="TX" | Foreman $40 | Journeyman $34 | Apprentice $22 | Tradesman $20 | Burden 2.0

Tab 6 -- EQUIPMENT SALES BUYOUTS

24 blank "XXXX" template rows + a Freight row, all zero on this job. For equipment Climate Pros purchases and resells rather than installs itself -- not used here.

Tab 7 -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM

Not a calculation -- a purchase order. Every cell pulls from PIPE 2.0 and rounds up to the nearest 20 feet, ready to hand to a supplier.

C15 =CEILING('PIPE 2.0'!Q23*$R$4, 20) -- round the piping tab's footage up to the next 20ft

Fields for Job Site Address, Order POC, Pickup/Delivery date exist but are blank in this sample.

Tab 8 -- HVAC

Same labor-hours-by-function layout as the refrigeration side (Mobilization, Duct Install, Demolition, Startup...). Entirely zero -- no HVAC scope on this job. The tab exists in every workbook regardless of job type.

Tab 9 -- TAX TABLE

Static: all 50 states x (Existing Construction / New Construction) → tax category text. Texas + Existing Construction (this job is a Remodel) = "SALES TAX LABOR & MATS." This is what SUMMARY!K4's XLOOKUP reads.

Tab 10 -- BidCalc

The final rollup. Pulls SUMMARY's Labor and Materials cost, re-derives sell price the same way, adds its own extra line items, applies tax, and produces one number.

Construction Material (=Materials)SUMMARY$127,364.43

Post Construction Warranty1.01%$5,502.99
Travel Expensestyped$68,114.29

Full walkthrough: the 70 MD cases, start to finish

Why this walkthrough
The 70-cases number is the most consequential figure in this bid, and the one traceable through every tab it touches with fully verified formulas.
1
Read the scope letter
Source: Refrigeration Scope letter
A dozen scattered bullets across departments, each in a different unit (feet/units/doors). No single sentence adds up to 70.
2
Estimator types 70 into DISPLAY CASES row 21
SUMMARY!C21/D21 -- typed by hand, no formula
The one un-auditable step in the whole chain.
3
Formulas convert 70 into $ and hours
SUMMARY!F21, G21, H21
Material: 70 x $130 = $9,100 | Pipefitter: 70 x 7 = 490 hrs | Case-setter: 70 x 5 = 350 hrs
4
Hours land in the task table, entirely as Overtime
SUMMARY rows 101-102
427 case-setter + 565 pipefitter hrs placed under OT -- because "all work is night work."
5
All task hours sum to Total Hours
SUMMARY!G121
2,384.34 total, 1,946 of them OT.
6
Hours split, priced with burden + OT loading
SUMMARY rows 123-131, 160-165
TOTAL LABOR COST (all roles/tasks): $195,217.12
7
Cost becomes Sell via reverse-margin
SUMMARY!I170
$195,217.12 ÷ (1-0.25) = $260,289.49
8
Rolls into BidCalc
BidCalc row 43
Now inseparable from every other bucket -- part of the $550,294.80 Total Bid.

Mismatches: Drawings/Uploads vs. Excel Findings

Every place I found where the Price Sheet and the Uploads either disagree, or where the Price Sheet contains a number with no traceable source in the Uploads at all.

#Excel locationWhat Excel showsWhat Uploads/Drawings showType of mismatch
1SUMMARY, Display Cases row 2170 MD cases @ 12' ($9,100, 490 pipefitter hrs, 350 case-setter hrs)Scope letter describes case changes in feet, discrete units, and doors -- no stated conversion between units, no sentence sums to 70Unverifiable aggregation -- estimator judgment with zero audit trail
2SUMMARY, Evaporators row 348 Commercial coils ($240, 96 hrs)Only 5 explicit "change out evaporator" bullets in the scope letterPartial mismatch -- 5 of 8 confirmed, 3 inferred from new-case installs (not stated anywhere)
3PIPE 2.0, SYS BR19 / BR2040 LF + 40 LF piping priced, real pipe sizes (0.625"/0.875")No matching system ID or sentence found anywhere in either letterUntraceable line item -- real dollars with zero source document backing
4SUMMARY C189, Sales Tax rate8.25%, drives $41,519.93 of the final bidNot in any Upload -- typed after a manual Google search (see the HYPERLINK note in the cell itself)Source lies entirely outside the document set -- single point of failure if the search result is wrong or stale
5Display Cases row 28 "Remove" vs row 21 "MD Cases"72 removed vs. 70 installed -- a gap of 2Scope letter describes two 5-case Kysor groups being replaced by fewer, wider Hussmann RLN sectionsExplainable gap, but not provable from the text -- logged as plausible inference, not a confirmed match
6PIPE 2.0, columns Q-AF (Copper/Insulation cost lookup)Real $/LF and part numbers present for suction/discharge sizing and insulationCould not confirm from either the tab's own layout or the Uploads whether this block is a per-system or per-pipe-size aggregationInternal ambiguity -- flagged as an open item rather than a guessed answer
What this list means for the platform
Items 1, 2, and 5 are cases where a human aggregated multiple prose bullets into one number using judgment that leaves no written trail -- exactly the "reasoning lives in the estimator's head" problem the AI platform is meant to solve. Item 3 is a genuine data-integrity gap (a priced line item with no source at all) worth flagging to Climate Pros directly. Item 4 is a process gap (external data source, no verification step) that the new platform's "state drives tax lookup" design should close. Item 6 is my own research limitation, not a project finding -- worth revisiting with more time or someone who knows this tab's internal layout.

Terms this folder has

Burden, BidCalc, Responsibility Matrix, RD/RE/BASR, Sublet, and Scheduling Data/Dispatch are now covered once in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's actually new/unique to this folder:

TermMeaning
DX / SCO2 / TCO2 / GLYRefrigerant system types on this job: DX = direct-expansion (standard), SCO2 = subcritical CO2, TCO2 = transcritical CO2, GLY = glycol secondary loop. This job runs all three side by side.
XHP pipeExtra-High-Pressure pipe rating, used for the new 90-Bar TCO2 system -- priced differently than standard Type L copper.
SaddleA pipe support/clamp component with its own part number and quantity, ordered per pipe size on the COPPER_INSULATION_FORM.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
Ice Room Bagger / SIC circuitA self-contained ice-maker/bagger machine treated as its own fully independent refrigeration circuit -- its own dedicated rooftop condensing unit, not tied into any store rack. This job has four: SIC08, SIC09, SIC10, SIC11.
Three Fitting TestA QA clause from the spec manual: the owner can randomly pull up to 3 fittings per installed system for inspection. A single failure (scale, water spots, burrs, paint, tape) requires the contractor to remove and redo every contractor-installed fitting and pipework on the entire project, at no additional cost and no schedule relief. The harshest QA penalty found in any folder so far.
Order Form vs. Pricing Tab (two different waste factors)COPPER_INSULATION_FORM is a physical purchase-order tool, separate from the pricing tabs -- it rounds up to 20-ft increments and applies its own 80% "order %" buffer (cell R4), which is a completely different number from the pricing tab's 10% waste factor. The two aren't meant to reconcile 1:1.
PARTS tab's two totalsA trap worth remembering for future folders: PARTS!P11 ("Buyout Management Parts Cost") is a small, separate 22-row sub-list of floor bumpers and LED lighting -- it can show $0 while the tab's real grand total, three rows further down at PARTS!J378, carries the actual valve/hardware catalog total. Checking the wrong cell here looks identical to the tab being genuinely unused.

1.5 IN-HOUSE GC and SUBLET CONTRACTORS

1.6 Refrigerant pricing table -- an over-pricing gap, the opposite direction from every prior folder

EQUIPMENT tab refrigerant: 80 lbs + PIPE 2.0 piping refrigerant: 50.331 lbs + Cases installed/relocated x 5: 0 x 5 = 0 lbs + Evaporator coils x 3: 0 x 3 = 0 lbs = 130.331 lbs computed total

But 200 lbs was manually typed into the R-449A priced row -- 69.669 lbs more than the formula supports. That pushes the "default bucket" (R407F) negative: -69.669 lbs, which isn't physically possible. Total Refrigerant Cost = 200 × $10.25/lb = $2,050, which appears to overstate the real charge by roughly 53%.

The refrigerant table's default-bucket gap runs the opposite direction here
On other folders (e.g. Sam's Club 8250-1010) the default-bucket leftover was an under-assignment -- real pounds left unpriced at $0/lb. Here it's an over-assignment: more pounds priced than the piping/equipment formulas actually support, which is a different failure mode entirely and, if real, costs the customer money rather than costing Climate Pros margin.

Two rows named almost identically, computed completely differently
Row 104 "START UP" is a formula tied to case/coil/equipment counts (zero here, since none apply). Row 110 "STARTUP" is a flat, manually-typed 20 hours. Easy to conflate when skimming -- they are not the same line.
What the 8-hour "Pressure Test/Evacuate" line is actually standing in for
The spec manual's procedure is far longer than 8 hours of continuous work: charge to 300 psig with dry nitrogen (fluorinated gases explicitly banned as tracer gas), hold 24 hours within ±1 psig, then a triple vacuum pulldown -- 1000 → 500 → 300 microns -- held another 24 hours before refrigerant can be charged. The 8 hours priced is the active labor (connect gauges, verify, disconnect) around those two passive 24-hour holds, not the full elapsed duration -- consistent, not an underestimate.

Region "TX" per role, margin 25%. Blended rates: ST $68/hr cost → $90.67 sell; OT $85 → $113.33; DT $102 → $136. Shift Differential explicitly "N/A" (pay factor 0%, unused).

Formula bug Contingency's sell price ignores its own margin cell
See Pricing Logic Reference -- now confirmed on a fifth folder: the margin cell here is labeled 5%, cost is computed off it correctly, but sell is computed as cost ÷ 0.8 -- a hardcoded 20% divisor, equivalent to a flat 25% markup -- completely independent of the 5% cell.
Two different "warranty" lines, not one
The spec manual states the condensing units/rack heat exchangers themselves carry a 5-year manufacturer warranty from date of store opening -- but labor is only included in year 1; years 2-5 are parts-only. That's a separate thing from the price sheet's own $848.92 Fixed 1% Warranty line, which is Climate Pros' own workmanship markup, not a pass-through of the manufacturer's coverage. Not a conflict, just two different warranties worth not conflating.

1.13 Job Scope Notes & Exclusions

Row 197's scope bullets cite MEPR-1.1's Keynote #4 (roof curbs), MEPR-1.1's Keynote #6 (pipe hoods by GC), and R-1.2's General Note #1 (valves) directly, and state the piping insulation as 1" wall for both liquid and suction lines. All three drawing citations check out verbatim against the source keynotes -- see the Uploads section above. Row 200's Exclusions list is truncated to 7 of the standard 13 items (stops after "Roof, floor and wall openings") -- see Pricing Logic Reference for the full baseline list and why this is a different kind of variation than the 14-item outlier seen on Walmart 0344-1005.

Tab 2 -- EQUIPMENT

Only 3 of the ~32 fixed checklist rows are non-zero -- and two of them don't fit the scope:

ItemQtyMaterialLaborRefrigerant

Two checklist rows for servicing an existing rack, on a job with no existing rack
Every other row on this 32-item checklist (replace suction shell, install oil float, change TXV body, etc.) correctly shows zero -- all of them are existing-rack service tasks, and this job's 4 ice-room circuits have no shared rack at all. Rows 35 and 36 are the only exceptions: $330 and 4.5 hrs billed for "changing" a rack liquid drier and suction filters that don't exist in this scope. The 0.5 quantity on row 36 is itself unusual for a physical parts count. Nothing in any Upload mentions this work -- most likely a leftover entry carried over from a different job's price sheet.

Tab 3 -- PIPE 2.0

A 600-row system template; only 4 rows are filled in (Systems 1/4/7/10, tagged SIIC08/09/10/11). All 4 correctly show liquid 5/8" / suction 1-3/8", Type "L" copper -- matching R-0.0 exactly. Header figures: 920 LF pipe w/waste, Installation Factor 0.18, 83 install labor hours, Pipe cost $7,628, Insulation $2,373, HFG (hanging materials/fittings/gas) $4,500.45 at a 0.45 factor, Saddles $0, Total Materials $14,501.45.

SIC09 is entered 90 feet short of the drawing's own schedule
R-0.0's Refrigeration Pipe Schedule states equivalent lengths of 92 / 195 / 76 / 85 ft for SIC08/09/10/11. PIPE 2.0 enters 92 / 105 / 76 / 85 ft. SIC09 alone is short by 90 feet -- a 46% shortfall on that one circuit -- which understates pipe, insulation, and hanger cost specifically for it. This is the single clearest, most quantifiable discrepancy anywhere in this folder.
Installation Factor 0.18 doesn't match the template's own menu
The tab's documented dropdown (cells I4-I8) lists exactly five options: 0.20 "T Bar ceiling," 0.15 "Standard Remodel," 0.132 "New Store 1.5" rubber," 0.12 "New Store," and 0.10 "Underground" -- the last two not previously recorded in this reference tab's own notes. This job (tagged "NEW" construction) uses 0.18, which matches none of the five. It reads as a manual override outside the template's own menu rather than a selection from it.

Separately, the Condensate Drain Lines section (rows 9-19) has 80 LF of ¾" Type M copper entered -- but its cost column shows $0, and the adjacent Insulation (Stand-Alone Only) section is completely empty. A real physical quantity with no cost attached, in the same spirit as HEB 57's uninsulated condensate line but here it's the pipe itself.

Tab 4 -- PARTS

Not zero, despite the small "Buyout Management Parts Cost" sub-list (rows 9-30, floor bumpers/LED lighting) showing $0 at cell P11. The tab's real total, J378, is $8,181.88 -- 9 real line items, effectively one full valve package per circuit × 4, plus consumables:

QtyItemCost
4MKC-2 Dual Voltage (control module)$350.92
4ME19S250 5/8" solenoid valve, less coil$1,298.68
4DX Ball Valve 5/8" -- Mueller AQ17862c$206.64
4DX Ball Valve 1-3/8" -- Mueller AQ17866c$619.88
4Magnetic Check Valve 1-3/8"$1,402.92
304.25" PVC Saddle (60/box)$232.20
15Roof Top Strut Blocks -- Small$360.00
307/8" Cush Clamp$111.00
1215% Silphos brazing rod, 1/8"×1/8"×36"$3,599.64
TOTAL$8,181.88

One solenoid + one liquid ball valve + one suction ball valve + one check valve + one control module, per circuit × 4, lines up cleanly with R-0.0's valve notes. Worth noting: the tab's own internal "Sell" column total (K378) shows $0 even though cost isn't -- a dead/unused column within this tab, harmless since the real sell price is computed later off the aggregate Materials total, not this column.

A confirmed match, not a mismatch: the brazing rod meets spec exactly
The spec manual requires brazing filler of Type BCuP-5, copper-phosphorus alloy with 15% minimum silver content. The 12 sticks actually purchased here are labeled "15% Silphos" -- an exact match. One of the few places in this folder where a specific Excel line item can be checked directly against a specific spec clause and comes back clean.

Tab 5 -- LABOR RATES

TX row: $40/$34/$22/$20, burden 2.0x, dated 1/13/2026 -- the standard baseline described in Pricing Logic Reference.

Tab 6 -- EQUIPMENT SALES BUYOUTS

Entirely zero -- a "Freight" line exists (flagged qty 1) but totals $0. Carries its own fixed 8.5% tax rate (vs. this job's main 8.25%) -- same fixed default seen elsewhere, dormant here since nothing was actually sold as a buyout.

Tab 7 -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM

Not a pricing tab -- a physical purchase-order form, separate from PIPE 2.0's own pricing math. Rounds to 20-ft increments and applies its own 80% "order %" buffer (distinct from the pricing tab's 10% waste factor). This is what actually resolves the insulation-thickness question: the form orders 320 LF of 5/8" Type-L copper with 1" insulation, and 340 LF of 1-3/8" Type-L copper with 1" insulation -- confirming the job's real general-line insulation is 1" wall, matching the SUMMARY tab's own scope notes. The spec manual's ¾" figure applies only to the narrower cases (Unistrut clamp wrap, suction-line valves) -- two different callouts for two different things, not a conflict.

Tab 8 -- HVAC

Every row checked -- entirely zero, as expected for a refrigeration-only job. Its header block again shows the same field-label misalignment confirmed on four other folders (e.g. "BID DATE:" shows "Port Isabel," "SCOPE:" shows "Texas," "DRAWINGS DATE:" shows "New Ice Room") -- see Pricing Logic Reference, now a fifth confirmation of the same template artifact.

Tab 9 -- TAX TABLE

Static, unchanged. Texas row: Existing Construction → "Sales Tax Labor & Mats," New Construction → "Use Tax Mats Only" -- confirms the mechanism (Texas, with Arizona, is one of only two states where the tax category itself flips between new and existing construction), and confirms this job's header correctly resolved to the New-Construction branch.

Tab 10 -- BidCalc

Same 10-tab rollup structure. Total Bid = $84,890.87 (rounds to SUMMARY's $84,891 -- matches). Confirms the margin-vs-markup naming convention twice over in one folder: the 25% Labor/Materials margin shows here as 0.3333 (33.3%) Markup, and the 20% Rentals margin shows as 0.25 (25%) Markup -- both are markup = margin/(1-margin), just displayed differently, not an error. Rows 38-39 are genuinely blank spacer rows inside the SUM(G5:G42) range, not a hidden line item.

Full walkthrough: SIC09, the circuit that's short 90 feet

1
The drawing states the real length directly
R-0.0, Refrigeration Pipe Schedule
SIC09: Ice Room Bagger, 31,500 BTU, 5/8" liquid / 1-3/8" suction, equivalent pipe length 195 ft.
2
The estimator enters a different number into PIPE 2.0
PIPE 2.0!E26/K26 ("System 4," tag SIIC09)
105 ft entered for both the liquid and suction line length -- 90 ft short of the drawing.
3
That shorter length drives pipe, insulation, and hanger cost for this one circuit
PIPE 2.0!G26, M26, and the insulation-diameter lookup columns
Liquid pipe: 105ft x $0.12/ft = $12.60 Suction pipe: 105ft x $0.0195/ft = $2.05 At the drawing's real 195ft: would be $23.40 and $3.80 -- roughly 1.86x higher on both lines
4
The shortfall rolls silently into the tab-wide totals
PIPE 2.0!D4, N3
920 LF total pipe w/waste and $14,501.45 total materials both already reflect the shorter SIC09 entry -- nothing downstream flags that one circuit is 46% short of its own drawing.
5
Rolls into BidCalc's Pipe line and the final Total Bid
BidCalc, SUMMARY!I98
The final $84,891 sell price is fully traceable end to end -- it just includes a materially understated quantity on one of its four circuits.

What's unique about this folder

  • The same firm and the same reviewing PE as HEB 57 -- GPM Engineering Inc., stamped by R.S. Pitzer (Texas #65986) on both jobs' refrigeration sheets -- suggesting one specific engineer covers refrigeration scope across HEB's ice-room jobs regardless of who drafts the rest of the set. (Henderson Engineers, cited elsewhere in this doc, is a Sam's Club job -- a different chain, not a departure from an HEB norm.)
  • The Three Fitting Test -- the harshest QA clause found in any folder so far: one bad fitting out of a random 3-fitting sample means redoing all contractor-installed pipework on the entire project, at contractor cost.
  • PARTS tab's two-total trap -- a real research pitfall worth remembering: the small, obvious-looking sub-total can be $0 while the tab's real total, several rows away, carries the actual dollar figure.
  • An off-menu Installation Factor (0.18) -- the first folder where PIPE 2.0's install factor doesn't match any of its own template's five documented options.
  • A refrigerant-table gap that runs the opposite direction from every prior folder -- over-assignment instead of under-assignment, the first time this specific failure mode has been seen.
  • A truncated, not expanded, Exclusions list -- 7 of the standard 13 items, the first folder to shorten the boilerplate rather than add to it. (Joe V's #831, analyzed afterward, repeats this exact same 7-item truncation -- see Pricing Logic Reference.)

Folder: Joe V's #831 -- Garland, TX

26.05.19 -- New Store (Existing Building): a CO2 Cascade Split rack, 5 self-contained singles, 61 display cases, 20 evaporator coils, and a real walk-in complex. The largest and most complete Uploads set analyzed so far.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
CO2 Cascade SplitRack A's actual system type per its own equipment schedule: two separate refrigerant loops sharing the same rack -- R-744 (CO2) on the low-temp/frozen (LT) side, R-513A on the medium-temp (MT) side -- linked by 3 "Cascade Condenser Plate Heat Exchangers" that transfer heat between the two loops without mixing the refrigerants.
Porta PackAn EQUIPMENT-tab checklist category representing the rack's MT (R-513A) side as a purchasable line item -- distinct from the "1/4-10 HP Condensing Units" category used for the 5 standalone singles.
WICP / IDFWalk-In Control Panel / Intermediate Distribution Frame (data network closet). This job has 5 WICPs across 3 IDFs -- IDF-1 (Frozen Foods/Dairy), IDF-2 (Market Cooler, serving 3 WICPs at once), IDF-3 (Produce/Bakery).
Case prefix codesRAFF/RAMK/RADE/RAPR/RAMS/RASF = rack-fed circuits by department (Frozen Food, Market, Deli, Produce, Meal Simple, Seafood); SIFF/SIDA/SIMK = Single-unit equivalents (Frozen Food, Dairy, Meat -- "DA" is Dairy, not "Island Display" as guessed on a prior folder before this job's equipment schedule spelled it out); SCBW = Self-Contained Beverage/Water, fully independent plug-in units with zero field piping.
Refrigerant Disclosure ScheduleA fire/mechanical code compliance sheet (2021 IFC Section 608), distinct from a pricing schedule -- discloses each system's refrigerant type, safety classification (A1), and estimated charge, and drives which enclosed spaces require mandatory leak detection.
G-1.1 Responsibility MatrixUnlike every RM seen on prior folders (all issued by a refrigeration engineer), this one is a "G" (General/Architectural) sheet issued by the project architect, covering furnish/install responsibility for the whole store's fixtures and equipment -- refrigeration is just one section of it.

The Uploads -- 18 documents

All from DC Engineering -- a firm not seen on any prior folder. The store is "DFWSS 4 -- Store #831" (the 4th Joe V's Smart Shop built in the Dallas-Fort Worth area), architect pb2 architecture + engineering (Architect of Record Scott Broadbent). The price sheet's own header states the scope directly: "New Store - Existing Building."

DocumentWhat's in it
G-1.1
Responsibility Matrix		An architect-issued (not refrigeration-engineer-issued) store-wide furnish/install matrix. Two tables: general fixtures (checkstands, doors, ice machines/baggers) and refrigeration-specific items (rack, case line, walk-ins, piping, controls, refrigerant charge). Dense small-print table -- confident on structure and categories, less certain on every individual dot-to-column mapping.
Specifications Manual
(1,543 pages)		A complete CSI MasterFormat manual covering every trade for ground-up construction (Divisions 00-28), sealed by 4 named engineers (Architect, Electrical, Mechanical, Fire Protection -- no separate refrigeration seal). Division 20 (Refrigeration), pages ~605-735 (~8% of the manual), has 12 sections -- far richer than any prior folder's spec manual, including dedicated sections for Refrigerant Compliance, CO2 Cascade & Parallel Racks, Evacuation/Charging, Commissioning, Checklists, and Refrigerated Case Specifications. Section 20 61 00 explicitly requires hiring Rapid Recovery to recover R-22 from the existing H-E-B system on this site -- see the walkthrough below.
MEPR-1.0
Overall Roof Plan		The combined HVAC/refrigeration/gas/electrical roof plan. Identifies Rack A (18,350 lbs), Condenser A (3,920 lbs), and two smaller self-contained units SIDA03/SIDA04 (1,575 lbs each) -- Rack A alone weighs as much as roughly 68 of HEB 383's standalone units. Also notes a desuperheater (heat recovery for water heating) and gives exact condenser piping sizes: discharge (2) 2-5/8", return (2) 2-1/8", receiver equalization (1) 7/8".
R-1.0
General Notes & Symbols		The single most data-dense sheet in this doc: a full branching piping schedule for Rack A (trunk lines 3A/4A/5A down to ~40 individual circuits), Ball Valve / Suction Check Valve / Solenoid Valve schedules with real Henry/Superior/Mueller/Sporlan part numbers mapped to specific circuit tags, and 11 general notes confirming this is a decommissioning/remodel process (existing copper returned to H-E-B, existing EMS credentials required, Asset Retirement Form).
R-1.1
Partial Floor Plan (Section 1)		Physical case layout confirming every circuit tag spatially, plus new tags (RAPR, RAMS, RASF, RADE, SCBW, RPB) and a labeled "Remote CO2 Charging Station" -- the first physical confirmation of the CO2 architecture. 24 Keyed Notes, mostly the familiar OFCI split, plus condensate heat-trace and a maintainability rule requiring an accessible ball valve outside every case for future replacement.
R-1.6
Under Floor Plan (Section 2)		The underground trench network. Keyed Note 2 -- "Existing refrigeration trench to be reused" -- the first hard confirmation this "New Store" reuses existing site infrastructure. Includes a Refrigeration Pit Box Schedule and real dimensioned trench-run lengths.
R-2.0
Equipment Schedule		Confirms Rack A as Hussmann, "Joe V's Custom CO2/R-513A," System Application: CO2 Cascade Split, LT Group (4 compressors, -22°F) and MT Group (6 compressors, 18°F per this sheet -- see the Circuit Schedule discrepancy below), condenser with 12 fans (an exact match later confirmed in the price sheet), plus full specs for the 5 standalone singles (all R449A, all Hussmann).
R-2.1
Circuit Schedule		Every circuit individually specified. Confirms LT Group runs on R744 (CO2) and MT Group on R513A, with 3 "Cascade Condenser Plate Heat Exchangers" (81,717 BTU/hr each) as the actual thermal link between loops. States MT Group SST as 16°F -- a 2°F mismatch against R-2.0's 18°F for the same system. Also identifies 4 real walk-in boxes (Krack brand) and the SCBW self-contained beverage cases with zero field piping.
R-2.2
Refrigerant Disclosure Schedule		A code-compliance sheet, not a pricing schedule. Discloses Rack RA-LT at 500 lbs CO2 and Rack RA-MT at 1,100 lbs R-513A. States directly: "Existing systems have R-22 or R-404A, all equipment will be new" -- settling the new-vs-existing question explicitly. All 7 enclosed spaces get leak detection as a blanket policy.
R-3.0
Controls General Notes & Symbols		The most important sentence in the whole folder: "...maintain existing refrigeration operation and control (Micro Thermo or other) throughout the remodel process until changeover is complete." Explicit use of "remodel" and a named legacy controller brand. Also defines the 5 WICP zones and gives exact low-voltage cable specs (Belden model numbers by sensor type).
R-3.1
Controls Partial Floor Plan		Physical placement of dozens of case controllers, confirms WICP1/IDF-1 are co-located near Frozen Foods/Dairy, and a dedicated high-temp alarm strobe (HTAS). Repeats R-3.0's notes/symbols rather than introducing new ones.
R-3.5
Controls Enlarged Plans		Two zoomed details revealing IDF-2 (serving WICP2/3/4 together, above the Market Cooler) and IDF-3 (serving WICP5, above the Produce Cooler) -- completing the 3-IDF network picture.
R-4.1
Condensate Routing Plan		A precise, temperature-driven material rule: walk-ins discharging at or below 32°F get Type "L" copper with heat trace; above 32°F get plain Schedule 40 PVC. Also: drains never downsized from fixture to hub, and shared multi-coil drains get bumped up one pipe size.
R-4.2
Case Dimension Plan		Pure dimensioning -- no notes or schedules. A cross-check tool for verifying physical case-run lengths, not a new source of scope.
R-5.1
Details (fully active)		18 real construction details: hanger spacing by pipe size (60"/72"/96" tiers), a strict rule that case condensate drains must run independently (never teed together), a detailed pipe-labeling spec (1" block lettering, rack name + suction temp on headers), and PVC-sleeve-to-copper sizing lookups.
R-5.2
Details (mixed)		The Remote CO2 Charging Station detail (referenced from R-1.1) with a real parts list: Mueller service valves, a Superior 650# relief valve, a Ritchie CO2 gauge -- and a rule that this specific piping is not insulated. A few panels are stamped "NOT USED," including a "BBQ Smoker Temp Sensor Detail" -- confirming this library covers food-service options this store doesn't use.
R-5.3
Details (fully active)		Four refrigeration pipe vault variants (Craigg Mfg cover plates), a hard 6" minimum air-gap code requirement at every case drain, a size-based trap-fabrication rule, and a genuinely easy-to-miss detail: return/supply air sensor factory-vs-field-install responsibility flips between Compact Series and Gentle Air Series coils.
R-5.4
Details (mixed)		The full equipment-labeling specification (engraved plastic tags, exact letter heights by location, install date required on every label) plus CO2-specific ball valve/bypass details. One "NOT USED" panel: a "BBQ Smoker Temp Sensor Detail" (a duplicate/variant of R-5.2's).

1.5 IN-HOUSE GC and SUBLET CONTRACTORS

1.6 Refrigerant pricing table -- internally clean, but light against the code-disclosed charge

EQUIPMENT tab refrigerant: 1,500 lbs + PIPE 2.0 piping refrigerant: 727.87 lbs + Cases installed x 5: 61 x 5 = 305 lbs + Evaporator coils x 3: 20 x 3 = 60 lbs = 2,592.87 lbs computed total

Manually assigned: R449A 482.87 lbs, R513A 1,350 lbs, R744 (CO2) 760 lbs, plus a 0.0015 lb rounding dust in the default bucket -- these sum to 2,592.8715, an exact match to the computed total. Unlike HEB 383, this refrigerant table reconciles cleanly with no over- or under-assignment. Total Refrigerant Cost = $38,453.44.

Priced pounds vs. the code-disclosed charge -- a moderate, not alarming, gap
R-2.2's Refrigerant Disclosure Schedule states the rack's actual system charge as 500 lbs CO2 and 1,100 lbs R-513A. The price sheet assigns 760 lbs CO2 (+52%) and 1,350 lbs R-513A (+23%). These are two independent estimation methods (a top-down code disclosure vs. a bottom-up pipe-volume/equipment formula) that aren't required to match exactly, but a 52% gap on the CO2 side is worth a second look. The R449A side is a closer match: 482.87 lbs priced vs. ~430 lbs implied by R-2.2's three single-unit "est. system charge" figures (150+150+130).
