The existing-rack-service rows finally make sense here
The same three checklist rows (oil filter, main liquid drier, suction filters) that looked like stray leftover entries on HEB 383 -- a job with no existing rack at all -- are used here at qty=2 each, on a job now confirmed to genuinely involve an existing rack. That's a good cross-validation: the same rows track correctly with each job's actual existing-equipment status rather than appearing at random.

The "12 Fan" condenser line matches R-2.0's stated fan count (12) exactly, and the 5 condensing units match the 5 standalone singles (SIFF01/02, SIDA03/04, SIMK05) exactly. Porta Pack's 1,000 lbs and the condenser's 400 lbs are reasonably close to R-2.2's disclosed 1,100 lbs (MT/R-513A) and 500 lbs (LT/CO2) respectively -- see 1.6 above.

Tab 3 -- PIPE 2.0

150 circuit rows entered -- by far the richest PIPE 2.0 tab analyzed so far (HEB 383 had 4). Header figures: 6,960 LF pipe w/waste, Installation Factor 0.18 (the same off-menu value as HEB 383 -- now a 2nd folder, and the same estimator both times, suggesting this may be a real, just-undocumented option rather than a one-off override), 627 install labor hours, Total Materials $173,246.30.

The MT trunk lines are transcribed almost perfectly
Checked all ~30 circuits on Rack A's MT trunk (3A/4A/5A and every RA-branch) against R-1.0's own Piping Schedule: 29 of 30 match exactly on both pipe size and length. The lone exception is 4.02A, which is missing its liquid-line entry entirely and shows the wrong length. Also confirmed: condenser discharge (2×2-5/8") and return (2×2-1/8") sizes match MEPR-1.0's roof-plan callout exactly, and all 5 single-unit suction sizes (1-5/8") match the Ball Valve Schedule's system groupings exactly.
The LT/Loop 1 side has two confirmed errors
Circuit "1.2A" is entered at 45 ft; R-1.0 states 15 ft -- a 30 ft, 3x overstatement. Circuit "RAFF04" is entered with ½" suction / 30 ft; R-1.0 states ⅝" suction / 10 ft -- both the size and the length are wrong. There's also an extra "1.3A" tag in the price sheet with no corresponding row in R-1.0's schedule at all.
A likely (not fully certain) pattern on the Loop 2 branch segments
On roughly half of the ~18 short "2.xxx" branch/riser segments feeding the MT trunk, the liquid-line size matches R-1.0 exactly every time, but the suction size is entered one standard pipe size larger than scheduled (e.g. ⅛" entered where ⅜" is scheduled). This is specific and repeated enough that it doesn't look like random noise, but this section of the schedule is dense enough that it deserves a fresh re-check before treating every instance as a confirmed error.

Tab 4 -- PARTS

Total $42,893.12, sums cleanly (no hidden-total trap this time). The Mueller suction-line check valve part numbers match R-1.0's Suction Line Check Valve Schedule exactly -- A-17935 through A-17944 -- and quantities match exactly for 4 of 5 sizes checked against R-1.0's "SYSTEM NUMBERS" column:

Part #SizeQty purchasedSystems scheduled in R-1.0
A-179353/8"1515 circuits exact match
A-179361/2"1010 circuits exact match
A-179411-3/8"11 circuit (RA33) exact match
A-179421-5/8"66 circuits exact match
A-179432-1/8"31 circuit (RA32) scheduled -- 2 extra purchased

The DX Ball Valve and solenoid line items reference generic Mueller/Sporlan catalog part numbers, while R-1.0 specifies Henry or Superior ball valves with a "no substitution" clause. The exact same generic wording ("ME19S250 5/8" SOL LESS COIL") appeared verbatim on HEB 383's PARTS tab too -- this looks like a fixed company parts catalog rather than a per-project lookup, so it's likely not a real error, just a different naming layer.

Tab 5 -- LABOR RATES

TX row: $40/$34/$22/$20, burden 2.0x -- the standard baseline.

Tab 6 -- EQUIPMENT SALES BUYOUTS

Entirely zero -- not itemized despite the job's real scale.

Tab 7 -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM

Real order quantities across 11 pipe sizes of Type L copper (880 LF of 3/8" down to 540 LF of 3-5/8"), plus 280 LF of ¾" and 180 LF of 1" Type M copper -- the condensate-line material confirmed by R-4.1's temperature-driven material rule. Only 200 ft of heat tape was purchased against 460 ft of Type M line -- a partial, not full, allocation. Plausible if only some of that footage runs through sub-32°F spaces, but worth knowing it isn't a 1:1 match.

Tab 8 -- HVAC

Every row checked -- entirely zero, as expected for a refrigeration-only scope. Its header block shows the same field-label misalignment confirmed on five other folders (e.g. "BID DATE:" shows "Garland," "DRAWINGS DATE:" shows the full scope text, "REVISIONS #:" shows a raw date serial number) -- now a sixth confirmation, see Pricing Logic Reference.

Tab 9 -- TAX TABLE

Static, unchanged. Texas row resolves the same way as every other Texas folder: New Construction → "Use Tax Mats Only." The "NEW" toggle drives this regardless of the "Existing Building" language elsewhere in the scope.

Tab 10 -- BidCalc

Same 10-tab rollup structure. Total Bid = $768,138.47 (rounds to SUMMARY's $768,139 -- matches).

Full walkthrough: the Rapid Recovery gap

1
The spec manual names a specific required vendor for a specific required task
Specifications Manual, Section 20 61 00
"Contractor shall hire Rapid Recovery to recover R-22 from H-E-B systems... Rapid Recovery will transport all recovered R-22 refrigerant to H-E-B banking location." Not generic boilerplate -- a named vendor, a named task.
2
The existing system is independently confirmed
R-2.2, IFC 608.5
"Existing systems have R-22 or R-404A, all equipment will be new." Combined with R-1.0's decommissioning notes and R-3.0's "Micro Thermo... throughout the remodel process," there's no ambiguity that a real R-22 charge exists on site to be recovered.
3
The price sheet has a line item for exactly this, priced at zero
SUMMARY, Rentals
Rapid Recovery ($2,300 one circuit): $0
4
Nothing downstream catches it
Total Summary, BidCalc
The $0 flows silently into a fully-reconciled, cleanly-computed $768,139 total. There's no formula error here -- the number is just missing at the source.
5
Why this matters more than a typical line-item gap
--
This is the same category of omission this team's own reference notes cite as the cause of a real $300,000 loss on a prior job -- a refrigerant-handling requirement that's easy to skip because it doesn't show up as a case, coil, or pipe length anywhere on a drawing.

What's unique about this folder

  • The richest Uploads set analyzed so far -- 18 documents including the largest specification manual seen (1,543 pages) and the first architect-issued (rather than refrigeration-engineer-issued) Responsibility Matrix.
  • The first CO2 Cascade Split architecture -- one rack, two refrigerant loops (R744 LT / R513A MT) linked by plate heat exchangers, plus 5 standalone R449A singles -- the most complex refrigerant architecture in this doc.
  • A confirmed, named, required service priced at zero -- the spec explicitly names Rapid Recovery for R-22 recovery from a confirmed existing system, yet the Rentals line shows $0. The clearest real-money finding in this doc since the historical $300K loss this pattern echoes.
  • The best-verified PIPE 2.0 tab yet -- 150 real circuits, with the MT trunk lines matching the drawing schedule almost perfectly (29 of 30 exact), proving the earlier flagged errors are real, isolated mistakes rather than a general transcription problem.
  • "New Store - Existing Building" -- a distinct scope category, stated in the price sheet's own header, that explicitly reconciles what would otherwise look like a contradiction between the folder name and the drawings' repeated remodel/decommissioning language.
  • The off-menu 0.18 Installation Factor recurs -- now seen on a 2nd folder, both estimated by the same person (Jay Jarratt Jr), suggesting this may be a real but undocumented option rather than a one-off override.

Folder: Walmart 3112-1009 -- San Antonio, TX

26.05.22 -- CU04 TCO2 Upgrade: the smallest, most self-contained job analyzed so far. One rooftop CO2 (R744) condensing unit bolted onto an existing, operating store to feed 2 new Grab-N-Go cases and a chicken warmer -- not a remodel of the store's own refrigeration system at all. Everything in this folder ties out unusually cleanly, which makes the one real data-entry error easy to isolate.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
CU04 TCO2The project's own tag for the single new rooftop condensing unit: a Kysor Warren ED-010-040-CM-OXV-A, R744 (CO2), rated for 80 bar, contractor-installed but owner-furnished through supplier Carrier. "CU04" is this store's running equipment number for its 4th condensing unit, not a 4th unit installed on this job.
CO2 Charging StationA dedicated, ground-level (not roof-level) service point for charging the CO2 system, reached by piping that runs through a wall penetration at the truck well, protected by a bollard. Distinct from the condensing unit itself.
"90 BAR" pipe designationThe pressure-rated copper class used for every CO2 line on this job (in place of the standard "ACR" copper class used on R448A/R449A/R513A jobs) -- appears throughout PIPE 2.0 and has its own dedicated ordering block on COPPER_INSULATION_FORM.
Order %A COPPER_INSULATION_FORM-wide factor (here set to 80%) that deliberately orders less material than the full calculated quantity, to be trued up later -- a real, intentional purchasing buffer, not a rounding error.
RM1 Field Installed Components MatrixA second, equipment-specific responsibility matrix (beyond the standard Case Installation checklist) that explicitly marks Compressors, Compressor Capacity Controls, and Glycol as N/A to this scope -- confirming the job never opens or touches any existing rack.

The Uploads -- 1 combined 22-sheet Bid Set

Unlike every other folder, this one has a single PDF -- _3112.1009 SAN ANTONIO, TX - BID SET.pdf (22 pages) -- where each page is a full drawing sheet from a Revit-authored, multi-discipline bid set (architect Galloway & Company AE Services; MPW Engineering holds Electrical, Refrigeration, and Fire Protection). Every page's sheet number was confirmed directly from its own title block, cross-checked against the Drawing Index on the Cover Sheet -- all 22 accounted for, no gaps.

1.7 Scheduling Data

FieldValue
# of Fitters2
Duration1.42 weeks
R Dispatch34.34 ST / 151 OT hrs (81.5% OT)
The most overtime-heavy labor dispatch analyzed so far
81.5% of all dispatched hours are logged as overtime, not straight time -- the highest OT share of any folder checked. This matters because the workbook's OT/DT cost isn't just a flat rate premium: the underlying burden multiplier is 2.0x for ST hours, but the formulas add +0.5 for OT and +1.0 for DT on top of that base (effectively ~2.5x burden on OT, ~3.0x on DT). That's a real, consistent template mechanism -- just one that becomes financially material here because so much of this job's labor was entered as OT rather than ST.

Tab 7 -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM

A real order, entirely under the "90 BAR" (XHP) CO2-rated copper category rather than standard ACR copper: 280 LF of 1/2" and 120 LF of 5/8" (both already net of the tab's 80% "Order %" purchasing-buffer factor), plus a small saddle order (38.4 units of the 3.25" bucket -- also net of the same 80% factor; the raw calculated quantity in PIPE 2.0 is 48).

Template gap No insulation-ordering columns exist for the 90-BAR CO2 section
The standard ACR copper blocks on this same tab (Type L and Type K) each have adjacent Black/White insulation order columns built in. The "90 BAR" block that this job's CO2 lines actually use (rows 56-66) has only two columns -- regular-wall LF and XHP-wall LF -- with no insulation columns at all. PIPE 2.0 correctly prices $963.80 of insulation for this job's CO2 lines, but there's no corresponding line on the literal purchase order telling the supply house how much insulation to send. This looks like a genuine template gap for TCO2/CO2 jobs specifically, since the form was clearly built with standard-refrigerant (ACR) piping in mind.

Tab 8 -- HVAC

Entirely zero, as expected for a refrigeration-only job. Header block shows the same field-label misalignment confirmed on six other folders (e.g. "BID DATE:" shows "San Antonio," "SCOPE:" shows "Texas," "REVISIONS #:" shows the raw date serial 46080) -- a seventh confirmation of this template artifact.

Tab 9 -- TAX TABLE

Texas row: Existing/Remodel → "Sales Tax Labor & Mats," New → "Use Tax Mats Only." Since this job's toggle is REMODEL, the sell price is taxed on labor and materials both, at 8.25%.

Tab 10 -- BidCalc

Total Bid = $45,389.04, matching SUMMARY's rounded-up $45,390 exactly. Every bucket (labor by category, OT, materials, rentals, contingency, warranty, tax) ties out one-for-one with the SUMMARY tab.

Full walkthrough: the case-sizing error

1
The drawings specify 8FT cases
GA1, Refrigerated Case Legend
2x RDC1069.652, Hillphoenix QC-R-35, explicitly listed as "8FT" self-service multi-deck (Grab-N-Go).
2
The price sheet enters them as 12' cases
SUMMARY, Display Cases
C21 (8' column): 0 D21 (12' column): 2
3
The wrong per-unit rate gets applied
SUMMARY row 21, XLOOKUP against J21:P21
Charged: 2 x $130 (12' rate) = $260 Should be: 2 x $104 (8' rate) = $208
4
Net effect
--
A $52 material overstatement flows silently into Materials cost/sell and the final Total Summary. Labor hours are unaffected because the pipefitter/case-setter hour rates happen to be identical for 8' and 12' MD Cases (7 hrs / 5 hrs either way) -- only the material dollar amount is wrong.

Non-Itemized: CO2 Charge Station Parts$350 / 10 hrsScope matches R3.1's Charging Station (CO2) detail

What's unique about this folder

  • The smallest, most self-contained scope analyzed so far -- a single bolt-on CO2 condensing unit and 2 cases added to an otherwise-untouched operating store, not a remodel of any existing refrigeration system.
  • A single combined 22-page Bid Set PDF instead of many separate upload files -- every sheet number confirmed directly from its own title block against the Cover Sheet's Drawing Index, with zero gaps.
  • The cleanest-reconciling folder analyzed so far -- refrigerant type, piping schedule, and parts all tie exactly to the uploads, with only one confirmed dollar error (case sizing) rather than several.
  • The first "Order %" purchasing-buffer mechanism identified -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM deliberately orders only 80% of the calculated material quantity, a real and intentional design choice, not a rounding artifact.
  • The first confirmed template gap specific to CO2/TCO2 jobs -- the order form has no insulation-ordering columns for 90-BAR pipe, even though the estimate itself prices insulation for it correctly.
  • The most overtime-heavy labor dispatch seen -- 81.5% of hours logged as OT, making the OT/DT burden-increment mechanism (base burden +0.5/+1.0) financially visible for the first time.

Folder: Kroger DAL653 -- Fate, TX

26.06.09 -- New Store, ground up. By far the richest Uploads folder analyzed so far: a 143-page 90% CDs drawing set plus a 680-page CSI spec manual, versus the single combined bid-set PDFs seen on most other jobs. It's also the first price sheet that arrived password-encrypted at the file level (not just sheet-protected) -- the workbook only opened after the password was supplied directly by the user.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
Rack A / Rack B (SYS A / SYS B)This store's two parallel CO2 transcritical racks, each with its own suction/liquid main loop, gas cooler, and vent line -- the entire job is organized around this A/B split, in the Uploads and in every price-sheet tab.
Gas CoolerThe CO2-system equivalent of an air-cooled condenser (BAC T5DC2-CO2-202-12.4, 1182 MBH). "Air Cooled Condenser 12 Fan" on the EQUIPMENT tab is this job's line-item name for the same rooftop unit.
CC1 / CC2 / CC3Danfoss AK-CC 55 case-controller types: CC1 = single-coil case/walk-in without electric defrost, CC2 = multi-coil medium-temp case, CC3 = walk-in with electric defrost. Tagged on every case circuit across R1.1, E1.3C/D, and the EM-series.
Protocol / Proto-AireA Hussmann distributed remote-compressor architecture described in full in spec section 11 41 63 -- but this particular store's rack architecture is CO2, not Protocol. The spec section is boilerplate carried over from Kroger's master template ("some items specified in this Section will not be used on the Project"), not evidence this job actually uses Protocol units.
Direct Buy Refrigeration Copper Pipe ProgramKroger-specific purchasing arrangement (spec 00 21 13 / 00 43 27): the Owner directly supplies refrigeration pipe, fittings, brazing rods, and piping insulation through a named vendor (United Refrigeration Inc.); Climate Pros supplies everything else and installs. This explains why COPPER_INSULATION_FORM functions as a literal supplier order form, not just an internal estimating tab.
90 BAR / XHP (Gas Cooler piping)The CO2-rated pipe class used specifically for the RA/RB Gas Cooler supply and return runs (1.625" XHP copper) -- distinct from the ordinary "K" and "L" copper classes used on the rest of the rack piping.
Order %Same purchasing-buffer mechanism seen on Walmart 3112-1009: COPPER_INSULATION_FORM here is set to 0.8, ordering 80% of the calculated quantity up front.

The Uploads -- a 143-page drawing set, a 680-page spec manual, and a raw text dump

Uploads\ contains three items: 2026.05.04 - Kroger Fate (DAL653) - 90% CDs - Builidng Package.pdf (143 pages, confirmed 143-for-143 against its own embedded Sheet Index -- no missing sheets, a first for this analysis), 2026.05.05 - Kroger Fate (DAL653) - Project Manual.pdf (680 pages, text-native CSI spec book), and pkg.txt (a 27,890-line raw OCR/text-layer dump of the drawing set, not a separate document). One level above Uploads sits the price sheet itself, in its own Price Sheet\ folder.

EM7.0 also has genuine unresolved design questions left in the 90%-issue set verbatim ("DOES DANFOSS BUILD PANELS?").

A-series floor plansDirect callouts for a "6″ REFRIGERATION CHASE" and a "PAINTED PVC REFRIGERATION PIPING RISER, RE: RISD-36 & RISD-36A," plus a "COOLER PANEL WALL" legend item.
M-series (HVAC)Two project-wide general notes: "CONSOLIDATE REFRIGERATION AND OTHER UTILITY LINE DROPS... CONSTRUCT BOX AROUND" and "PROVIDE INSULATION AND UV TAPE FOR ALL EXTERIOR HVAC REFRIGERATION PIPE."
P-series (plumbing)Repeated "COOLER COOLING COIL CONDENSATE" routing keynotes for every refrigerated room. No water-reclaim content beyond ordinary condensate drainage.

Project Manual -- the 7 refrigeration-relevant CSI sections (of 46 total)

SectionWhat's in it
11 41 03 -- Prefabricated Insulated Wall PanelsThe walk-in cooler/freezer box shell itself: Kroger-supplied insulated panel walls and stainless cove base, contractor-installed, with insulation-core fire ratings. Sits one section before the range usually associated with refrigeration -- the architectural enclosure everything else hangs off of.
11 41 22 -- Refrigerated Fixture InstallationCase setting/leveling/sealing, Kroger's temp-tag and loose-thermometer requirements, walk-in evaporator coil hanging, strip curtains.

11 41 43 -- Refrigeration System InstallationThe core spec: Kroger Direct Buy Program furnish/install split, CO2-specific piping classes and insulation-thickness table, brazing/joint rules, a full evacuation/charging procedure (1000→700→300 microns), superheat setpoints, the Temperature Performance Chart, and a $1,500-per-event fine if nitrogen purge isn't used during brazing. No glycol content anywhere -- this store is DX/CO2 only.
11 41 46 -- EMS Controls InstallationDanfoss EMS install spec, furnish/install responsibility matrix, CO2-specific Controller Programming matrix referencing a "Kroger CO2 Setpoint File," and the 16-zone leak-detection alarm-threshold spec (>1000 ppm).
11 41 63 -- Electrical InstallationIncludes the "Protocol Refrigeration Equipment" article in full -- boilerplate for an architecture this store doesn't actually use -- plus arc-flash certification requirements.

Genuine cross-references found outside these 7 sections: 00 21 13 / 00 43 27 (Direct Buy Refrigeration Copper Pipe bidding procedure, naming vendor United Refrigeration Inc.); 00 72 14 General Conditions requiring "Refrigerant Management Documents" as a contractual closeout deliverable; 01 64 00 Vendor Contact List naming the actual OEMs (Danfoss, Copeland/CPC, United Refrigeration, KPS Global, Hussmann, Chase Industries); 07 72 13 Manufactured Curbs specifying heavier 2×6″ nailers for condenser curbs vs. 2×2″ for RTUs; 07 21 00 / 06 10 53 freezer-floor XPS insulation and HDPE thermal-break threshold specs. No PDF annotations exist anywhere in the 680-page manual -- it's a clean, flattened document.

1.6 Refrigerant

R744 CO2: 2,800 lbs @ $6/lb R407F: 641.499 lbs @ $0/lb = 3,441.499 lbs total, $16,800 total cost
641.499 lbs of refrigerant weight carries zero dollars
Only the 2,800 lbs of R744 is priced (at $6/lb = $16,800, the exact figure in "TOTAL REFRIGERANT COST"). The 641.499 lbs of R407F sits in the table with a real weight but $0/lb. R6.1's case schedule includes its own per-case "REFRIG" column for factory-charged refrigerant inside each case -- 641.499 lbs is a very plausible match for that column's total, which would make the $0 price intentional (factory charge, not a field fill Climate Pros buys). But nothing in the Uploads or the workbook states this explicitly, so it's flagged as a real gap rather than a confirmed non-issue.

Full walkthrough: the vector-only schedule that looked missing

1
R6.1 exists in the Sheet Index and the file
G0.2 Sheet Index, page 69 of the Building Package
The Sheet Index lists R6.1, "REFRIGERATION EQUIP. SCHEDULE," as sheet 4 of 4 in the R-series, and page 69 of the PDF is physically that sheet.
2
A text-only read comes back empty
page.get_text() on page 69
Characters extracted: 0
The entire schedule table -- every case row, every compressor spec -- is drawn as vector CAD line-work with no embedded text layer. An automated or keyword-search-based read of this sheet returns nothing, which is exactly the failure mode that led an earlier pass to flag R6.1 as "referenced but missing."
3
A rendered image shows it's fully populated
page.get_pixmap() at high zoom, page 69
Dozens of case rows across all four racks (ALT/AMT/BLT/BMT), full CO2 two-stage compressor data, percent-loaded figures (73-77%), and the gas-cooler spec are all visibly present -- nothing about the sheet is blank or a placeholder.
4
Three independent sources agree at "2 racks, 2 gas coolers"
R1.1, EQUIPMENT tab, E6.2
R1.1 plan: Condenser A + Condenser B tagged on the roof EQUIPMENT tab: Comp Rack qty=2, Air Cooled Condenser 12 Fan qty=2 E6.2 single-line diagram: CO2-A/CO2-B and Gas Cooler-A/Gas Cooler-B as named breakers = all three agree independently
5
Net effect
--
The lesson isn't a dollar error -- it's a methodology one: a vector-drawn schedule with no text layer will read as absent to any tool that only searches text, even when it's the most densely populated sheet in the entire set. Cross-checking against the price sheet's own quantities (and a second, independent electrical schedule) is what actually confirms the sheet is there and correct.

What's unique about this folder

  • The richest Uploads folder analyzed -- a 143-page drawing set plus a 680-page spec manual, versus a single combined bid-set PDF on most other jobs.
  • The first encrypted price sheet -- the workbook was password-protected at the file level (OLE2/CFB container with EncryptionInfo/EncryptedPackage streams), not just sheet-protected; it only opened once the password was supplied directly.
  • A real embedded shadow takeoff -- 141 hand-placed PDF annotations by the job's own estimator sitting directly on the piping drawing, a second independent source for the PIPE 2.0 circuit lengths.
  • The clearest example yet of a vector-only sheet masquerading as "missing" -- R6.1 returns zero characters from a text extraction despite being the most densely populated sheet in the set, corrected only by rendering and cross-checking against three independent sources.
  • A complete sheet set, for once -- 143 sheets in the Sheet Index, 143 sheets in the PDF, zero "referenced but missing" gaps -- unlike several other folders analyzed.
  • Kroger's own Direct Buy Refrigeration Copper Pipe program -- a distinct owner-supplies-materials arrangement (pipe, fittings, brazing rod, insulation) documented nowhere in the refrigeration-specific spec sections themselves, only in Division 00's bidding procedures and the Division 01 vendor list.
  • Zero glycol, confirmed at every level -- absent from the spec text, priced at qty 0 in the PARTS catalog, and consistent with the CO2-only rack architecture found throughout the drawings.

Folder: Walmart 5102-258 -- Gretna, LA

26.06.08 -- Remodel with an "SCO2 Upgrade." The most document-rich folder of the Walmart CapX/remodel jobs analyzed so far: 16 separate Uploads files (a responsibility matrix, 14 single-sheet refrigeration/electrical drawings, and a 40-page spec) all built around adding one new Secondary CO2 pump skid onto two existing R407A racks -- not a new store, not a new rack, just a new secondary loop bolted onto what's already there.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
SCO2 / LTA SCO2"Secondary CO2" -- the drawings' own System Type field defines it as "CO2 Liquid Overfeed / Parallel DX": a secondary loop where liquid R744 is pumped by a RAE Corp skid, evaporates in the SCO2-tagged cases, and is re-condensed by a heat exchanger fed from the existing DTA DX rack's suction group. Not a transcritical/subcritical CO2 booster rack, despite the "SCO2 Upgrade" job title.
LTA CONN: 1 / LTA CONN: 2The two taps off the existing DTA DX rack's header that cool the new secondary skid. Connection 1 carries both suction and liquid to the skid; Connection 2 carries suction only.
"LTA DX" / "MTAS DX" (Compressor Schedule)Legacy/drafting labels for DTA DX's own Suction Groups #1 and #2 -- capacity cross-check confirms these aren't separate racks, but the "LTA DX" name is easy to confuse with the unrelated "LTA SCO2" skid.
Type K vs. Type L copperTwo ACR hard-drawn copper wall-thickness classes. Spec 15600 calls for Type L on R744 liquid-overfeed lines 1-3/8" and smaller, Type K only at 1-5/8" and larger. This job's price sheet uses Type K uniformly across every SCO2 circuit regardless of size -- see the walkthrough below.
U-Bend Bypass (SCO2)A temporary supply-to-return jumper installed during phased cutover so a CO2 circuit, case, or walk-in can be tested/evacuated/filled before its final connection -- specified only for the CO2 side, with no DX equivalent in spec 15600.
Battery Backup (BB-2)A Generac PWRCELL2 cabinet/inverter/transformer assembly serving the LTA SCO2 skid -- on this job, the cabinet/inverter/transformer are existing equipment being augmented with new battery modules, not installed fresh.

The Uploads -- RM1, 14 drawing sheets, and a 40-page spec

All 16 files share Job No. 765727-01, Store No. 05102-258, Proto 41. Consultant MPW Engineering (Refrigeration EOR Scott C. Webb, Electrical EOR Aaron B. Morse). Unlike Kroger DAL653, every drawing sheet here has a real, readable text layer -- except R4/R4.1, which look blank under a text-only read but actually carry dense OEM submittal content as embedded raster images.

SheetWhat's in it
RM1 -- Responsibility MatricesFour tables: KE2/RE2 case-controller install (38 lines), Pump Skid install (38 lines, including CO2-specific and unused-glycol-boilerplate items), Case Install (45 lines), and the headline Field-Installed-Components matrix (Existing Racks vs. SCO2 Skid columns). Legend: RC/GC/BAS/OEM/PS/WMT/AES/WTC/MC.
RD1 -- Demolition PlanTwo existing roof racks (ETR DTA DX, ETR MTB DX) with demo/relocate case tags. 24-48hr post-shutdown filter/drier replacement requirement if any rack is opened.
R1 -- Overall PlanAdds the new "NEW LTA SCO2 (ON ROOF)," a battery-backup callout, and a new CO2 charging station. "Existing refrigeration piping may be re-used unless otherwise noted."
R1.1 -- Enlarged PlanFull case-tag layout, DX and SCO2 side by side. LTA CONN: 1/2 taps shown. 32 keynotes, several CO2-specific (isolation check valves, pump skid install, riser traps, spare stubs).
R2 / R2.1 / R2.2 -- SchedulesThe core equipment data: three Refrigeration System Schedules (DTA DX, LTA SCO2, MTB DX), a Pump Skid Schedule (RAECorp WMT-SEC-CO2-1-10), a Compressor Schedule, a Refrigerant Charge Schedule, an SCO2 Phasing Schedule, Piping Schedules, and a ~100-row Skid Parameter Schedule.
R3 / R3.1 / R3.2 -- Details21 standard details total -- generic DX pipe-support standards, then the CO2-specific package (skid piping connections, roof penetration, charging-station sign text, U-bend bypass, isolation check valves). Several labeled "(SCO2)(GLYCOL)" -- shared templates even though this job has no glycol.
R4 / R4.1 -- SubmittalsText-only extraction returns almost nothing ("INFORMATION ONLY / NOT TO BE SIGNED") -- but both carry real embedded-image content: full Hussmann rack data sheets for both existing racks, and the RAE Corp CO2 skid unit drawing with dimensions, clearances, and a 4,848 lb shipping weight.
RE1 / RE1.1 / RE1.2 -- ElectricalRoof plan (panels CHA1/CHA2/CHB off MSB), enlarged per-circuit wiring, and the CO2 Skid Backup Battery & Inverter Wiring Diagram (Generac PWRCELL2, 9 kWh, existing equipment augmented with new battery modules).
15600 -- Spec (40 pp.)UniSpec Section 15600, Refrigeration Systems. Schedule I insulation table (R744 liquid-overfeed: Type L at 1-3/8" and smaller, Type K at 1-5/8" and larger). Full DX and CO2 evacuation/charging procedures. The U-bend bypass procedure. Pages 25-40 are unlabeled exhibits (AHRI recovery-cylinder tables, handover forms, an RFI flow chart) -- entirely fluorocarbon-focused; CO2/R-744 never appears in the recovery/banking exhibits, and the CO2 execution article has no "Recovery and Removal" subsection at all.

Confirmed cross-document oddities worth carrying into the estimate review: the Trans-critical CO2 and Secondary/LOF CO2 valve-pressure specs in 15600 are word-for-word identical (90 bar/1305 PSIG) -- likely copy-paste, questionable for a subcritical secondary loop; the CO2 evacuation-pump spec says "50 microns or more" where the parallel DX clause correctly says "or less"; "BASR plans and one-line diagram" is cited on RE1/RE1.1 but not included in this Uploads set.

Confirmed defect A negative refrigerant quantity, on a live bid
"TOTAL LBS" is the straight arithmetic sum of every row in the table, including the -934.997 R407F line -- which very nearly cancels the +935 lbs manually entered into R448A (off by 0.003 lbs). "TOTAL REFRIGERANT COST" ($17,865.02) matches exactly 935×13.6 + 858.17×6, meaning the negative R407F row contributes $0 despite carrying negative weight -- so the dollar total is unaffected. But a bid document showing -934.997 lbs of refrigerant is a real, visible defect in the template's "shift the balance to Type as needed" mechanic: typing a real quantity into R448A drove the R407F "default" cell negative instead of to zero.

Circuit-by-circuit takeoff matching the drawings' own tags: the LTA SCO2 header row, every 1A/1.1A/.../A01-A20 SCO2 case circuit, LTA CONN 1 (17 ft, 1-1/8" liquid/2-5/8" suction) and LTA CONN 2 (17 ft, 2-5/8" suction only, no liquid) -- an exact match to R2.1's Piping Schedule -- CO2 CHRG ST (160 ft of 1/2" suction-only line to the charging station), then MTB DX and DTA DX header rows with their own DX circuits (B02-B08 DX, 1.1AS-AS11 DX). Nothing populated past the last real DX riser row -- the rest of the 600-system template is blank.

Every SCO2 circuit uses Type K copper, regardless of size
Spec 15600's own Schedule I table calls for Type L on R744 liquid-overfeed lines 1-3/8" and smaller, reserving Type K for 1-5/8" and larger. Every SCO2 circuit on this sheet -- down to 0.5" and 0.625" lines -- is entered as Type K on both liquid and suction. Every DX circuit on the same sheet correctly uses Type L at equivalent small sizes. See the full walkthrough below.

Tab 7 -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM

Order % factor 0.8, same purchasing-buffer mechanism as other folders. Type L block (DX side): 700 LF of copper across six sizes, plus 520 LF of Black insulation ordered against it. Type K block (SCO2 side): 2,200 LF of copper across eight sizes -- matching every SCO2 circuit in PIPE 2.0.

Template gap, third confirmed instance Type K's insulation columns are labeled White, not Black
This job's SCO2 piping is insulated Black throughout (confirmed in PIPE 2.0, $8,630.40 priced). But the Type K copper block's adjacent insulation-order columns are headed "WHITE INSULATION" -- so none of the CO2 secondary loop's insulation has a matching line on the literal supplier order form, because the only columns available next to Type K copper are for a color this job doesn't use. Walmart 3112-1009 hit a version of this same gap (no insulation columns at all for XHP pipe); here the columns exist but assume the wrong color. Saddle quantities (Black, net of the 80% order factor) and the unused XHP/90-BAR block are both correct.

Full walkthrough: the copper type that drifted from spec

1
The spec sets a size-based rule
Spec 15600, Part 2, Schedule I
R744 Liquid Overfeed CO2: pipe 1-3/8" and smaller = Type L hard-drawn copper; pipe 1-5/8" and larger = Type K. The HFC/DX rows carry no such size-based type split.
2
Almost every SCO2 circuit is well under 1-3/8"
R2/R2.1 Piping Schedules, R1.1 plan
The individual case circuits (A01-A20 SCO2, 1.1A-1.41A SCO2, etc.) run mostly 0.5"-0.875" liquid and 0.5"-2.125" suction -- squarely in the "should be Type L" band by the spec's own rule.
3
PIPE 2.0 prices every one of them as Type K anyway
PIPE 2.0, columns F/L (Copper Type)
A07 SCO2: 0.5" liquid = Type K, 0.625" suction = Type K A16 SCO2: 0.5" liquid = Type K, 0.5" suction = Type K every SCO2 circuit on the sheet: Type K
Meanwhile every MTB DX/DTA DX circuit on the same sheet correctly alternates and uses Type L at equivalent sizes.
4
The order form confirms it wasn't a one-off entry
COPPER_INSULATION_FORM
The Type K block is fully populated with 2,200 LF across eight sizes, matching the SCO2 total -- not a stray row, a consistent choice across the whole CO2 scope.
5
Net effect
--
Type K is thicker-walled and more expensive than Type L, so this deviation runs conservative/safe rather than short -- but it's a real, consistent departure from what the governing spec calls for. It also has a second-order consequence: because Type K's insulation-order columns are White-only on this form, none of the SCO2 side's $8,630.40 of (Black) insulation has a matching purchase-order line at all.

What's unique about this folder

  • The clearest "bolt a secondary CO2 loop onto an existing DX rack" job analyzed -- LTA SCO2 is cooled by taps off the existing DTA DX rack (LTA CONN: 1/2), not a standalone booster system, despite the "SCO2 Upgrade" name.
  • A confirmed, systematic copper-type deviation from the governing spec -- every SCO2 circuit uses Type K regardless of size, where spec 15600 calls for Type L below 1-5/8". Conservative in direction, but consistent enough (fully mirrored on the COPPER_INSULATION_FORM) to be a deliberate estimating choice, not a slip.
  • A negative refrigerant-pounds line on a live bid -- the first folder where the "shift the balance to Type as needed" refrigerant mechanic visibly breaks, producing -934.997 lbs of R407F (net $0 cost impact, but a real display defect).
  • The new record for overtime-heavy dispatch -- 81.6% of hours logged as OT, edging out Walmart 3112-1009's prior 81.5%.
  • A confirmed reference-table gap, not an estimator error -- LABOR RATES has no Louisiana row at all, so this Gulf Coast job runs on Texas rates by necessity.
  • A third confirmed instance of the CO2-insulation order-form gap -- Type K's insulation columns are White-only, leaving this job's Black-insulated SCO2 piping with no matching purchase-order line.
  • Two sheets that read as blank but aren't -- R4/R4.1's text-only extraction returns almost nothing, but both carry dense, real OEM submittal content (Hussmann rack data sheets, RAE Corp skid unit drawing) as embedded images -- the same "vector/image trap" pattern first confirmed on Kroger DAL653's R6.1.

Folder: United #0510 -- Hereford, TX

26.06.05 -- Remodel, Produce/Meat/Dairy. The smallest, plainest Uploads set analyzed so far: no formal refrigeration schedule (R1/R2-style) at all -- just a 2-page prose scope-of-work letter and a hand-marked-up FF&E floor plan. That plainness is exactly what makes this folder's gap real: without a schedule to check against, the only way to find what the scope letter left out is to trace the plan's own color-coded markup system item by item.

Full walkthrough: the "C" tags the scope letter never mentions

1
The scope letter describes 8 systems, and only 8
0210-510-Refrigeration Scope.pdf
A5, A7, A8, B1, B3, B9, B10, B11 -- every refrigeration line item in the bid traces to one of these eight tags.
2
The marked-up plan carries a second, unexplained tag series
510-REMODEL PLAN Mark Up_01.pdf
19 "C##" tags (C18 through C36) are scattered across the store -- present only on the marked-up sheet, not in D101/X101's base drawing, and never referenced anywhere in the scope letter.
3
15 of the 19 land directly on refrigeration equipment
Same sheet, rendered at high zoom
C18-C21: frozen-food island cases (RLNI back-to-back) C22: Meat Freezer walk-in C23-C24: dual-temp frozen coffin cases C25: Deli Freezer walk-in C26: Frozen Cakes case C27: Bakery Freezer walk-in C29: Bakery Vault (cooler) C33-C34: Produce Prep Vault, Produce Vault C35-C36: Service Deli case, Deli Retail Case = 15 of 19 "C" tags are real refrigerated equipment
4
A separate embedded punch-list image adds even more
D101/X101's raster "510 REMODEL NOTES" image
Invisible to a text-only read, this image lists refrigeration items with no scope-letter counterpart at all -- most notably "Add 4' Pure Cold end cap cooler between aisles 1 and 2" -- alongside an open, unresolved question: "Ask Joe about freezer vault doors."
5
Net effect
--
Nothing in the price sheet traces to a "C" tag or to the punch-list image -- both exist purely in the Uploads, disconnected from the eight-system scope letter this bid was actually built from. Whether that's because those items are genuinely out of scope, already covered elsewhere, or simply missed, isn't resolvable from these documents alone -- but the gap between "what's marked on the plan" and "what the bid prices" is real and traceable.

What's unique about this folder

  • The plainest source documentation analyzed so far -- no formal refrigeration schedule at all, just a prose scope letter and a hand-colored merchandising floor plan standing in for one.
  • The first folder where the primary evidence is a color-coded legend rather than a CAD schedule -- NEW/SURPLUS, PRIORITY 2, DEMO, RELOCATED, EXISTING, REMOVED, each system tag matched to a colored zone and a real case model number.
  • A genuine, traced documentation gap -- 15 of 19 "C" tags on the marked-up plan sit directly on refrigerated equipment with zero mention in the scope letter the bid was built from, plus a punch-list image (invisible to text extraction) naming a specific missing item ("4' Pure Cold end cap cooler").
  • A third consecutive folder above 80% overtime -- 81.7% here, following Walmart 5102-258's 81.6% and Walmart 3112-1009's 81.5%, now reading as a real pattern rather than a one-off.
  • A confirmed non-issue used as a control case -- System A5's $0 piping cost is exactly what the scope letter's "use existing line sets" note predicts, a clean example of the Uploads and Excel agreeing.
  • Confirmed negative on the crane/RTU line -- the scope letter's general boilerplate item has no roof plan, no RTU, and no crane cost anywhere in this job, correctly reflected by SUMMARY's $0 crane rental.

Folder: HEB Corp #271 -- West Columbia, TX

26.05.25 -- "Refrigeration Upgrade & Merch Remodel." By far the largest Uploads folder analyzed: a 94-sheet full construction drawing set plus a 1,229-page master spec book (of which roughly 165 pages are actually refrigeration-relevant). One new multiplex rack ("RACK RA") replaces an entire legacy R-22/R-404A/R-407A system store-wide, running R-449A exclusively -- and the documents themselves spend a surprising number of pages talking about a refrigerant this store doesn't use.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
RACK RAThe single new Hussmann custom multiplex rack replacing the entire legacy system -- one physical rack with separate LT and MT suction groups (not two racks), 88 total HP, R449A, 15,700 lbs, ground-mounted on a new dedicated concrete pad.
WICPWalk-In Control Panel -- the field enclosure wiring together case controllers, sensors, door alarms, and leak detectors for a group of walk-in boxes, reporting back to the RDM Data Manager.
RDMHEB's case-controller/EMS network standard (RDM Data Manager, RDM IDF) -- every case tag on this job routes to it; its physical cabling is specified separately, in the low-voltage communications division, not in the refrigeration spec sections.
"NOT USED" CO2 detailsA recurring pattern across this drawing set: dedicated CO2/R-744 construction details (charging station, ball valves) exist in the shared HEB detail library but are explicitly crossed out "NOT USED" on this specific job -- see the walkthrough.
Three Fitting TestSpec 20 63 95's QC mechanism: HEB may cut out up to 3 random field-brazed fittings for inspection -- if even one is found oxidized/failed, the entire project's pipework and fittings must be re-done.
KV (Key Valve)The plumbing symbol for a shutoff valve at a refrigerated case's water connection (misting/ice systems), formally defined on P-4.1's legend.

The Uploads -- a 94-sheet drawing set and a 1,229-page spec book

Project: H-E-B West Columbia, Corp #271, 110 Brazos Street. Refrigeration/MEP Engineer of Record: Erdman Anthony (David Graser, PE). Approved refrigeration installers list (appearing on both R-1.0 and M-0.1) includes Climate Pros (Mike Palmer) by name, alongside Hussmann, CoolSys, and others.

Sheet(s)What's in it
DG-1.1 / G-1.1 -- Phasing PlansA 5-phase demo/rebuild sequence (Phase 0-4) with the store required to stay ≥50% operational per department throughout. "START UP NEW RACK RA - MEDIUM TEMPERATURE SUCTION GROUP" (PH-1a), "...LOW TEMPERATURE SUCTION GROUP" (PH-3b).
Q-0.0 / Q-1.0 -- Responsibility Matrix / Equipment PlanCases, condensers/coils/compressors, and the EMS are H-E-B-vendor-supplied; warranty on condensers/compressors and EMS is split "HEB/Contractor." Full case schedule -- all Hussmann (ID5SL multidecks, RL frozen reach-ins).
DR-1.1 / DR-2.0 -- Demolition Plan & Schedule~21 individual circuits on the legacy rack, all marked REMOVE, running R-404A, R-407A, and R-22 -- a full legacy-refrigerant retirement, not a like-for-like swap. Demo general notes require copper turned in to HEB's Returned Goods Center and an Asset Retirement Form within 72 hours. DR-2.0 itself is a different page size (612×792 pt Letter) than every neighboring sheet (3024×2160 or 792×612) -- a real production/export inconsistency in the PDF, not a content gap.
A-2.0 -- Enlarged Cooler/Freezer PlansThree 1/4"=1'-0" enlarged plans: Dairy Cooler, Frozen Food Freezer, Produce Cooler, each dimensioned with a 50' wall-mount hose-reel keynote. "REPLACE COOLER/FREEZER PANELS IN SAME LOCATION," insulated door/strip-curtain keynote, and "REFER TO Q SHEETS FOR EQUIPMENT TO BE REINSTALLED."
R-1.0 through R-4.1 -- General Notes, Floor Plan, Schedules, Controls, CondensateThe core equipment record: every disclosed circuit and the RACK RA nameplate itself confirm R-449A, with zero exceptions. RACK RA: 88 HP, MCA 360A/MOPD 400A, LT group (3 compressors, 90.3 MBH) + MT group (4x model 4PES-12, 439.6 MBH), condenser model MXF-10. Heat-traced condensate (Chromalox SRF, 5W/ft) in freezer/cooler departments.
R-2.2 -- Refrigeration Disclosure SchedulesThe IFC 605 compliance table names R-513A and CO2 (R-744) alongside R-449A -- but every actual disclosure row on the same sheet lists only R-449A. See the walkthrough.
R-5.1 through R-5.4 -- Refrigeration Details21+ construction details. Two dedicated CO2 details -- "Remote CO2 (R744) Charging Station Detail" and "CO2 Ball Valves w/ 3/8" By-Pass" (x2) -- are explicitly stamped "NOT USED." Detail 8's engraved-tag example correctly reads "RACK A +22F SUCTION HEADER" / "RAMK01 R449A EEV."
S-1.0 / S-1.1 -- Slab Plan / Foundation DetailsEquipment weight schedule confirms RACK RA at 15,700 lbs, new, on a new concrete slab (detail 7/S-1.1) -- not the existing slab.
A-4.1 - A-4.8 -- Architectural DetailsExterior walk-in flashing/gutter/corner-closure, interior slab/threshold details by adjacency type, and a dedicated "Refrigeration Chase Detail" (A-4.5) plus "Individual Narrow Island Case Chase Detail."
E-2.1 / E-4.1 / E-6.1-6.2 / E-7.1 / E-8.1 -- ElectricalTwo 400A/3P/208V disconnects feed RACK RA directly off the main 1000A/1600A switchboard via new Panel RB. Refrigeration loads spread across 9 panels (largest: Panel RB, 76,360 VA). E-8.1 shows the full RDM network topology back to "RDM IDF #1." Naming trap: the demo one-line (DE-4.1) shows an existing electrical panel that is also named "RA" -- a coincidental match to "Refrigeration Rack RA," entirely separate equipment.
P-series -- PlumbingKey-valve (KV) locations at every case, a produce misting-system schematic, ice-maker/bagger filtration schedule (Howe filters, 1,000-6,000 lb/day), and a 12" minimum separation rule between underground refrigerant and waste/water lines. P-2.1 also shows a domestic hot-water recirculation pump mounted directly above a refrigeration box.
Fire Protection (DFP-1.0, FP-1.1)Coolers/Freezers/Sales Floor classified Ordinary Hazard Group 2 for sprinkler design. Dry-pipe risers required above freezers with insulated arms "to prevent condensate build-up rolling down into sprinkler trigger" -- fire protection rules directly shaped by the refrigeration equipment. No ammonia/CO2 detection interlock anywhere, consistent with R-449A.
Spec 13 21 26 -- Walk-In Coolers and FreezersBox construction spec; 10-year parts warranty on panels/boxes, 2-year on electrical hardware.
Spec 20 61 00 through 20 90 00 -- the core Division 20 refrigeration sections (~130 pp.)Full evacuation stairstep (1000μ→500μ→300μ, 24-hr final hold, ±100μ drift max), 300 psig nitrogen pressure test held 24 hrs, brazing at 15%/45% silver by joint type, the Three Fitting Test, leak alarm thresholds (500 ppm halocarbon), and full RDM controller setpoint tables.
Spec 20 63 92 -- CO2 Cascade & Parallel Compressor Racks (15 pp.)Zero store-specific references anywhere in the section -- pure generic boilerplate for whichever HEB stores actually run CO2. Closing line reads "END OF SECTION 23 62 13" -- wrong CSI division entirely, a copy-paste artifact.
Spec 02 41 00 -- DemolitionDetailed refrigerant-retrieval pipe-capping tolerances by equipment type, plus HEB's own Refrigerant Management Reclaim Program (United Refrigeration Inc., named contact Bruce Campbell, Verisae work-order process, cylinder part numbers, pallet-loading rules).
Spec 20 61 00 -- Refrigerant Compliance (named-vendor find)Names a separate, specific R-22 recovery vendor -- "Rapid Recovery," contact Chad Fancher -- distinct from the URI reclamation vendor in 02 41 00. This is the same name as SUMMARY's $4,500 "Rapid Recovery" rental line (Tab 1.9): the rental line isn't a generic label, it's this named subcontractor.
Spec 28 44 00 -- Refrigerant Detection and AlarmMonitors "R-407C, R-449A." Two alarm levels, distinct from the rack-level 500 ppm threshold elsewhere: Level 1 = 50 ppm (low-speed ventilation, blue strobe), Level 2 = 250 ppm (high-speed ventilation ≥5 min, amber strobe). Sensors at all coolers/freezers, wall-mounted at 60" AFF.

Confirmed negative: PDF annotations across both files are essentially just PE-seal stamps (no redlines, no substantive markup) -- except one incidental find: page 8 of the spec book's own Seals section still carries a leftover header from a different HEB project ("H-E-B EDNA, CORP #351... Plan Mod #20"), a template-reuse artifact rather than a scope issue. Several spec cross-references are internally malformed ("Section 2060100," "Section 25 6395 Appendix A") -- a sloppy citation layer sitting on top of otherwise solid technical content. Section 20 70 00 (Refrigerant Conversion) describes converting an existing system's charge in place, which doesn't match this store's overall demo-and-replace scope -- though R-2.0's own appliance schedule notes that several retained existing condensing units (SIFF04, SIBW05, SIFF06, SIDA07) were themselves converted to R449A from their original charge, so the section isn't purely inapplicable, just mismatched to the rack itself. Its own page headers still read the unfilled placeholder "XX XX XX."

Tab 3 -- PIPE 2.0

Header: 496 install labor hrs, 4,960 LF pipe w/waste, 406.4585 lbs piping refrigerant, $124,435.29 total materials (Pipe $66,677.20 + Insulation $16,725.90 + Saddles $3,671.84 + HFG $37,360.35) -- all tie to SUMMARY exactly.

Circuit-by-circuit data ties to R-1.1's pipe sizing schedule to the inch: 1A (1-3/8" liquid / 3-1/8" suction, 85 ft), 2A (7/8" / 2-5/8", 238 ft), RADA04 (1/2" / 1-3/8", 32 ft), plus the full RAFF/RAMK/RAPR/SIDA01/SIFF03/SIMK02 circuit list -- real data ends cleanly at System 79, the rest of the 600-system template is blank.

Every liquid line is insulated, contradicting the scope's own stated exception
The job scope explicitly distinguishes "Non-Subcooled LQ lines (not insulated)" from "Subcooled LQ lines (insulated w/ 1" wall)." But every single liquid-line row in PIPE 2.0 carries 1" Black insulation -- including small branch circuits like RAFF16 (0.375" liquid). This is confirmed independently on COPPER_INSULATION_FORM (Tab 7): the Type L copper and Black insulation columns show identical linear footage, size for size, with no non-insulated segment anywhere. Either the entire rack's liquid header really is subcooled end-to-end (which would make this correct), or the estimate blanket-insulated every liquid run regardless of the split the scope itself describes -- a real, cross-confirmed item worth a second look, not a stray data entry.

Tab 4 -- PARTS

Total $15,460.03, tracing to 16 catalog lines: solenoid valve coils/bodies (MKC-2, ME9S240/250/270), 8 sizes of DX ball valves (3/8"-2-1/8", $3,721.36), 4 sizes of magnetic check valves ($1,690.45), 25 units of 15% Silphos brazing rod ($7,508.25 -- again the largest single line), and 125 ft of heat tape + connector/splice kits ($951.10 -- matches R-4.0's Chromalox SRF condensate heat-trace requirement directly).

Tab 5 -- LABOR RATES

Standard, unchanged. TX row.

Tab 6 -- EQUIPMENT SALES BUYOUTS

Entirely zero -- placeholder "XXXX" rows throughout.

Tab 7 -- COPPER_INSULATION_FORM

Order % factor 0.8. Type L copper populated across 10 sizes (140-720 ft each); Type M (condensate) at 120 ft of 3/4" + 80 ft of 1" with matching black saddle quantities. Type K and XHP/90-BAR both correctly zero -- no CO2 anywhere, confirmed again at the order-form level. As noted in Tab 3, the Black insulation columns match the copper columns foot-for-foot across every size -- the same 100%-liquid-line-insulation pattern, independently confirmed here.

Tab 8 -- HVAC

Entirely zero. Same recurring header field-label misalignment (BID DATE shows "West Columbia," SCOPE shows "Texas," DRAWINGS DATE shows the scope text). Note that the RTU demo/relocate/new-set labor tracked in SUMMARY's Non-Itemized section (Tab 1.4) is real, priced work -- it's just booked under refrigeration, not here.

Full walkthrough: the CO2 store that isn't

1
The real equipment is unambiguous: R449A, no exceptions
R-2.0 / R-2.2 schedules
Every circuit schedule row and the RACK RA nameplate itself list R-449A. Not one piece of actual, disclosed equipment on this project runs anything else.
2
Yet CO2/R-513A surfaces repeatedly across the drawings
R-2.2, R-3.0, R-5.2, R-5.4
The IFC 605 compliance table names R-513A and CO2 (R-744) as project refrigerants; the case-controller refrigerant-code key includes "21 if CO2"; two dedicated CO2 construction details exist -- a Remote CO2 Charging Station and CO2 Ball Valves w/ 3/8" By-Pass.
3
Both CO2 details are explicitly crossed out
R-5.2 detail 6, R-5.4 details 3 & 7
Each one is stamped "NOT USED" directly on the sheet -- the CO2 content is present in the shared HEB detail library, but deliberately not part of this specific store's scope.
4
The spec book confirms it independently
Spec 20 63 92, CO2 Cascade & Parallel Compressor Racks
Store-specific references found in this 15-page section: zero "RACK RA" mentioned: no "West Columbia" mentioned: no Closing line: "END OF SECTION 23 62 13" (wrong division)
Pure generic boilerplate, confirmed by its own copy-paste error at the very end.
5
Net effect
--
The price sheet gets this right -- zero CO2 parts, zero R-513A/R-744 anywhere in EQUIPMENT, PIPE 2.0, or PARTS, matching the store's real R449A-only scope. But a reader working from the drawings or spec alone, without cross-checking the actual equipment schedule, could easily walk away thinking this store has a CO2 component. Four independent documents (two drawing sheet types, one spec section, and its own internal typo) all point the same direction: this is leftover shared-template content, not real scope -- worth flagging to the client if a fully "clean" record set matters to them.

What's unique about this folder

  • The largest folder analyzed by far -- 94 drawing sheets plus a 1,229-page spec book, dwarfing even Kroger DAL653's 823 combined pages.
  • Climate Pros named directly in the Uploads, on two separate sheets (R-1.0 and M-0.1), as one of HEB's approved refrigeration installers for this store.
  • A confirmed, multi-document "boilerplate refrigerant" pattern -- CO2/R-513A content surfaces on four independent documents (two drawing sheet types plus a dedicated 15-page spec section) despite the store running R-449A exclusively, with every CO2-specific construction detail explicitly marked "NOT USED."
  • A real, cross-tab-confirmed insulation question -- 100% of liquid lines priced with 1" insulation despite the scope's own text distinguishing subcooled (insulated) from non-subcooled (not insulated) runs.
  • The estimator's own company appears as a sublet line item -- "CLIMATE PROS" billed at a distinct 15% margin for exterior pipe jacketing, an internal-allocation pattern not seen on any other folder.
  • The clearest structural tie-out yet -- RACK RA's exact weight (15,700 lbs) and its dedicated new concrete pad are independently confirmed on the structural sheets (S-1.0/S-1.1), not just the refrigeration set.
  • The richest refrigerant-logistics documentation of any folder -- HEB's own Refrigerant Management Reclaim Program (named United Refrigeration Inc. contact, Verisae work-order process, cylinder part numbers) plus the "Three Fitting Test" QC mechanism, both from the Demolition spec section.
  • A fourth consecutive job in the 81-82% overtime band -- 81.6% here, consistent with Walmart 3112-1009, Walmart 5102-258, and United #0510.
  • A rental line item confirmed as a real named vendor -- spec 20 61 00 names "Rapid Recovery" (contact Chad Fancher) as HEB's designated R-22 recovery company, matching SUMMARY's $4,500 "Rapid Recovery" rental line word for word -- the first folder where a generic-sounding rental label is directly traceable to an actual named subcontractor in the spec.
  • A second confirmed equipment/panel naming collision -- an existing electrical panel is also named "RA," coincidentally identical to "Refrigeration Rack RA," the same style of naming trap first flagged on Walmart 5102-258 ("LTA DX" vs. "LTA SCO2").

Folder: Whole Foods 10593 -- Frisco, TX

26.05.26 -- New Store Build-Out. The first folder in this document running a genuinely fully transcritical CO2 booster system (R744), rather than a cascade, secondary, or hybrid arrangement -- two racks, two adiabatic gas coolers, two heat reclaim tanks, all confirmed store-specific equipment against a 1,204-page spec book that turns out to spend most of its refrigeration-adjacent page count on sections that have nothing to do with the CO2 system at all.

Terms this folder has

Universal terms already covered in the Pricing Logic Reference tab. What's new here:

TermMeaning
Rack DTA / Rack MTB ("Rack A" / "Rack B")The two transcritical CO2 booster racks. DTA is dual-temp (SG1 at -22°F serving freezers/blast chiller/ice flaker; SG2 at 25°F serving dairy/produce/meat prep; 8 compressors). MTB is a single medium-temp group at ~18°F (4 compressors). 12 CO2 compressors total.
Gas CoolerThe transcritical-system equivalent of a condenser -- rejects heat from the high side, but above the CO2 critical point (31.1°C/88°F) it cannot fully condense the refrigerant, so the plant relies on a downstream flash tank/valve instead. Two BAC TSDC2-CO2-202-12.4 adiabatic units here, one per rack, roof-mounted.
Charge SaverRoof-mounted Mueller receiver vessel pair (one per rack), each on its own dedicated 25A/3P circuit on isolated-ground Panel CP -- reduces the refrigerant charge the rack itself has to hold.
High Side / Low Side (per spec §239100)Not a location, a pressure-class definition: High Side = components exposed to MT/parallel-compressor discharge pressure (rated 130 bar / 1,885 psi); Low Side = everything else (rated 45 bar / 653 psi).
XHP / 90 BAR / 120 BAR pipeMueller Extra High Performance (or Wieland K65) copper-iron tubing, the only pipe class permitted on CO2 high-side runs -- min. 90 bar rated for intermediate pressure, min. 120 bar for high pressure. Ordinary ACR tubing is explicitly disallowed.
Flash TankPressure-reducing vessel sitting between the high-pressure zone (gas cooler discharge / transcritical operation) and the intermediate-pressure zone (down to the LT/booster compressor suction) -- mentioned once in the spec (valve pressure-class definition) but never given its own setpoint or sizing anywhere in the 56-page CO2 install spec, despite the section's title.

The Uploads -- Bid Set drawings, a 1,204-page spec book, and two annotated single-page excerpts

Five documents. The 111-page Bid Set (refrigeration/electrical sheets only, pages 40-111 reviewed) confirms the two-rack, two-gas-cooler, two-heat-reclaim-tank system in full schedule detail. No CSI spec-section numbers are cited anywhere on the drawings -- the only cross-referencing possible is by content, not by citation.

Sheet / areaWhat's in it
R100/R200 -- Refrigeration SchedulesRack DTA (SG1 -22°F/141.9 MBH + SG2 25°F/237.5 MBH, 8 comps) and Rack MTB (~18°F/474.9 MBH, 4 comps); full Hussmann/Bohn-CO2/Hill Phoenix/Howe/Traulsen case-circuit schedule for both racks; two identical BAC gas coolers (~5,000 lbs each).
Electrical (Panels RP1/RP2/CP)RP1 (400A, ~64.3 kVA, mostly refrigeration), RP2 (400A, lightly loaded), CP (100A isolated ground -- carries every controls/leak-detection/Charge Saver circuit). Racks on dedicated 480V feeders separate from the 208V case panels; refrigeration on generator-backed ATS-2.
Leak detection (two independent systems)Parasense "Freeway" (16 zones, R744A/Safety Group A1, 1,250/3,750 ppm auto LLSV/EEPR isolation thresholds) and a Bacharach/Toxalert machine-room monitor (TLV-TWA low, 500 ppm high, full compressor/valve shutdown at high).
Referenced-but-missing sheetsM302 (mechanical sequence of ops for purge fan/dampers), EM200/EM220 (Parasense leak-zone and energy-monitoring schedules), R-102 (heat-tape coolers/freezers reference) -- confirmed genuinely absent from the 111-page set by a full-text cross-check: each name appears only as a callout on some other sheet ("REFER TO M302...", "SCHEDULE ON SHEET EM200"), never as its own title-block page.
PIPING SPEC-WF FRISCO.pdf (2pp, image-only)An estimator-annotated excerpt of spec §239100 pages 10-11 -- the exact pressure-class/insulation/brazing paragraphs, red-circled by hand.
WF-R200 FRISCO.pdf (1pp, image-only)The same R200 schedule sheet, with a second red-annotated callout: a "CO2 Refrigeration Straight Pipe and Fitting Requirements" table giving exact design/max pressure by line type -- see the walkthrough.
Furniture Specification Package (12pp)Confirmed irrelevant to refrigeration -- a store fixture floor plan showing Hill Phoenix case models/lengths by aisle, useful only as a case-count cross-check.

The Specifications book (1,204 pages) required the most filtering of any folder yet -- see the walkthrough for how a whole-book keyword sweep for "refrigerant"/"CO2" surfaces over 20 CSI sections, only one of which (§239100) is actually about this store's CO2 system:

SectionTitle / pagesWhat it actually is
223530Heat Reclaim Tanks (627-631)The DHW-side storage tank (Lochinvar, 150-psig, copper U-tube exchanger) -- the counterpart to the CO2 rack's factory-installed heat-reclaim exchanger. Internally mis-titles its own cross-referenced piping section ("Refrigeration Piping" vs. the real "Refrigerant Piping").
232300Refrigerant Piping (807-813)Unrelated -- comfort-cooling split-system/RTU DX piping only (500 psi max, single-pass 35°F-indicator evacuation). Zero technical overlap with the CO2 racks.
238126Split System Air-Conditioners (897-904)Unrelated -- HCFC-free/A2L flammable-refrigerant comfort-cooling equipment spec (ASHRAE 15, UL 60335-2-40). Sits immediately before §239100 and was missed entirely on the first research pass -- see the walkthrough.
239100WFM Trans-Critical CO2 Installation Specification, R744 (905-960)The real one -- the sole technical home for pressure classes, evacuation/charging, warranty. See Tab breakdown below.
284600Fire Detection and Alarm (1185-1204)Standard fire-alarm scope; zero reference anywhere to Parasense/Bacharach/CO2/leak-detection, despite §239100 requiring a fire-alarm tie-in verification at startup. Also carries leftover existing-building remodel boilerplate ("test the existing system," "maintain existing service") inappropriate to this ground-up new store.

Two factors overridden from template defaults, both traceable to the CO2 scope
Installation Factor = 0.18 -- doesn't match any of the five presets (.20 T-Bar ceiling / .15 Standard Remodel / .132 New Store 1.5" rubber / .12 New Store / .10 Underground); the closest preset for a "New Store" would be .12. A manual bump, plausibly justified by CO2 XHP-pipe/mezzanine/rooftop installation complexity, but undocumented as such anywhere in the workbook. HFG Factor = 55% -- well above a typical default, but this one is self-documented: the sheet's own note reads "HFG = Hanging Materials, Fittings and Welding Gas, Increase Factor when using XHP Pipe/Material." The clearest example yet of a factor override the workbook explains in its own words.

Two high-pressure copper categories exist on this form -- only one is used
The form offers both an XHP column and a dedicated 90 BAR column for high-pressure CO2 tubing. XHP is populated (1-3/8"=240, 1-5/8"=300, 2-1/8"=160 LF); the 90 BAR column is entirely zero across every size. This job's transcritical piping is being tracked exclusively under the XHP designation. Unlike the recurring 3-job "template gap" pattern (insulation ordered with no matching copper, or vice versa), this isn't a gap -- the Black insulation figures at those same three sizes match the XHP footage exactly, foot for foot, confirming the insulation is correctly keyed to the pipe actually being used.

Full walkthrough: three sections, one keyword, only one is real

1
A whole-book keyword sweep for "refrigerant"/"CO2"/"trans-critical" returns 20+ hits
Full 1,204-page text scan, by CSI section
Sections from 000110 (project narrative) through 271500 (low-voltage) all trip at least one of these keywords somewhere in their text -- far too broad a net to hand-read in full.
2
Two of the largest hits turn out to be entirely unrelated comfort-cooling content
§232300 (807-813), §238126 (897-904)
232300 "Refrigerant Piping" governs split-system/RTU DX piping (500 psi max, old-style single-pass evacuation) -- zero crossover with the CO2 racks. 238126 "Split System Air-Conditioners" is a generic HCFC-free/A2L flammable-refrigerant spec for comfort-cooling equipment. Both use the word "refrigerant" repeatedly; neither is about this store's food-refrigeration system.
3
238126 sits immediately before the real CO2 spec -- and was missed on the first pass
Page 897 vs. page 905
The first research pass, given a page-range hint of 905-962 for the CO2 installation spec, never opened pages 897-904 and had no reason to suspect an entire extra section was wedged in immediately before it. An automated section-boundary sweep run afterward mis-attributed pages 897-960 entirely to Section 238126, because §239100's own title page buries its "SECTION 239100" header below a large decorative cover block, past the point a simple "does SECTION appear near the top of the page" heuristic was checking. Manually reading page 905's actual text was what caught it.
4
Only one 56-page section is genuinely the CO2 system's spec
§239100, pages 905-960
Every pressure class, insulation requirement, brazing rule, evacuation/charging procedure, warranty term, and commissioning checklist for the actual transcritical CO2 racks lives here and nowhere else in the 1,204-page book.
5
Net effect
--
A reader keyword-searching this spec book for "refrigerant" or "CO2" -- rather than reading section titles -- would land on at least three unrelated sections before finding the 56 pages that matter, and could easily mistake 238126's A2L-refrigerant flammability language for something relevant to the CO2 racks. This is the inverse of HEB Corp #271's "boilerplate CO2 inside a non-CO2 store" trap: here the store genuinely is CO2, but the spec book still buries the real section inside several pages of unrelated comfort-cooling content using the same keywords.

Mismatches: Uploads vs. Excel

What's unique about this folder

  • The first genuinely fully transcritical CO2 (R744) booster job in this document -- distinct from Kroger DAL653's secondary CO2, Walmart 5102-258's SCO2, Sam's Club 8250-1010's two standalone RCUs, and Joe V's #831's CO2 cascade split.
  • Two racks totaling 12 compressors, both priced under a repurposed "Comp Rack" line since no dedicated CO2-rack taxonomy exists -- the same pattern now confirmed across every CO2 folder in this document.
  • 121 real CO2-rated ball valves purchased, including two sizes rated to 140 BAR -- exceeding the spec's own 90-120 bar minimum, the most explicit material-exceeds-spec confirmation seen on any folder.
  • A self-documented factor override -- the PIPE 2.0 HFG Factor bumped to 55%, with the sheet's own note explaining why ("Increase Factor when using XHP Pipe/Material") -- the clearest example yet of a template override the workbook explains in its own words, alongside an undocumented Installation Factor override (0.18) that isn't.
  • Confirmed genuinely-missing referenced sheets via full-text cross-check, not just visual scan -- M302, EM200, EM220, and R-102 each appear only as a callout on another sheet, never as their own page, across the entire 111-page Bid Set.
  • A spec book that buries its own real section -- an automated section-boundary sweep mis-attributed 64 pages to the wrong CSI section number because the real section's header sits below a decorative cover page, catchable only by manually reading the actual page text (see walkthrough).
  • Two entirely unrelated comfort-cooling spec sections (232300, 238126) share the exact keywords ("refrigerant") that a search for the CO2 content would use -- the inverse of HEB Corp #271's "boilerplate CO2 in a non-CO2 store" trap.
  • A one-directional fire-alarm/leak-detection interlock gap -- the CO2 spec requires a fire-alarm tie-in at startup; the fire alarm spec itself never mentions refrigerant leak detection.
  • A leftover R404A reference inside the R744 leak-detection attachment, and a new-store fire-alarm spec still carrying existing-building remodel boilerplate -- both template-reuse artifacts, the same family as HEB Corp #271's leftover "H-E-B Edna" header.
  • Breaks the four-job 81-82% overtime streak -- 13.1% OT here, versus Walmart 3112-1009/5102-258/United #0510/HEB Corp #271 all clustering at 81.5-81.7%.
  • Estimator's own hand-annotated excerpts among the Uploads -- two image-only single/double-page PDFs (a spec excerpt, a schedule sheet) each red-circled at exactly the pressure-class data that ended up justifying the 140-BAR valve purchase in PARTS.
